## Comprehensive Exploration Report: CrewAI Platform Service Accounts, RBAC, and Worker Configuration

Based on a thorough exploration of the kix-services-crewai repository, here are my findings organized by topic area.

---

### 1. Service Account Configuration

#### **Kubernetes Service Account (K8s SA): `crewai-sa`**

**Location:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/serviceaccount.yaml`

**Key Details:**
- **Name:** `crewai-sa` (set via values: `serviceAccount: "crewai-sa"`)
- **Creation:** Created by Helm template in PreSync wave **-35** (earliest wave)
- **Workload Identity Annotation:** `iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
- **Used By:** web, worker, buildkit, replicated pods

**Configuration in values.yaml (lines 37-42):**
```yaml
serviceAccount: "crewai-sa"  # Created by Helm PreSync wave -35

# Workload Identity binding for GCP service authentication
workloadIdentity:
  gcpServiceAccount: "crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
```

**Token Automount Settings:**
- **Web:** `automountServiceAccountToken: true` (line 57)
- **Worker:** `automountServiceAccountToken: true` (line 159)
- **BuildKit:** `automountServiceAccountToken: true` (line 238)

---

#### **GCP Service Account: `crewai-platform`**

**Location:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/service-accounts.tf`

**Resource Details:**
- **Account ID:** `crewai-platform`
- **Email:** `crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com`
- **Created:** Inline in Terraform (not via module) to avoid circular dependencies with HMAC keys

**IAM Bindings (lines 21-104):**

1. **Cloud SQL Access** (platform-data project):
   - `roles/cloudsql.client`
   - `roles/cloudsql.instanceUser`

2. **GCS Bucket Access:**
   - Data bucket: `roles/storage.objectAdmin`
   - Logs bucket: `roles/storage.objectCreator`

3. **Artifact Registry Access:**
   - Shared repository (`crewai-images`): `roles/artifactregistry.reader`
   - Crew builder repository (`crewai`): `roles/artifactregistry.writer` + `roles/artifactregistry.reader`

4. **Secret Manager Access** (security project):
   - `roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor`

5. **Workload Identity Binding:**
   - Binds K8s SA `crewai/crewai-sa` to GCP SA via `roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser`
   - Member: `serviceAccount:kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.svc.id.goog[crewai/crewai-sa]`

6. **GitHub Actions Impersonation:**
   - `roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator` for GitHub CI/CD

---

#### **Crew Namespace Service Account: `crewai-crews-sa`**

**Location:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf`

**Resource Details (lines 66-75):**
- **Name:** `crewai-crews-sa`
- **Namespace:** `crewai-crews`
- **Token Automount:** `false` (security hardening - pods must explicitly mount if needed)
- **Created by:** Terraform (not Helm)
- **Purpose:** Service account for crew workload pods

---

### 2. RBAC Configuration

#### **Current RBAC Implementation**

**Finding:** There is **NO** Role or RoleBinding defined for the platform service account (`crewai-sa`) to manage resources in the `crewai-crews` namespace.

**Existing RBAC (only for post-install job):**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/rbac-post-install-setup.yaml`

This creates a **temporary** service account (`post-install-setup-sa`) with limited permissions:
- **Resources:** configmaps (get), deployments (get/list/watch), replicasets (get/list/watch), pods (get/list), pods/exec (create)
- **Scope:** `crewai` namespace only
- **Lifecycle:** PostSync hook with `BeforeHookCreation` delete policy (temporary)
- **Currently disabled:** `postInstallSetup.enabled: false` (line 507)

#### **Expected RBAC (per CREW_NAMESPACE.md documentation)**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/docs/CREW_NAMESPACE.md` (lines 52-61)

The documentation states the platform SA should have:

**In platform namespace (`crewai`):** Limited permissions (ConfigMaps, Secrets read-only)

**In crew namespace (`crewai-crews`):** Full management permissions:
- Full Pod management (create, delete, exec, logs)
- ConfigMap and Secret management
- Deployment, StatefulSet, Job management
- Service and PVC management

**Reference RBAC file mentioned:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/rbac/crew-access-role.yaml` - **NOT FOUND in this repository** (appears to be legacy documentation)

---

### 3. Worker Pod Configuration

#### **Purpose**
**From values.yaml line 156:** "Background job processor (Sidekiq) for async tasks and crew provisioning"

#### **Configuration**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml` (lines 158-227)

**Key Settings:**
- **Service Account:** Uses `crewai-sa` (inherited from global settings)
- **Token Automount:** `true` (line 159) - **CRITICAL: Enables K8s API access**
- **Resources:**
  - Requests: 500m CPU, 1Gi memory
  - Limits: 2000m CPU, 4Gi memory
- **Autoscaling:** 1-5 replicas based on 70% CPU / 80% memory
- **Security Context:**
  - `runAsNonRoot: true`, `runAsUser: 1000`
  - `allowPrivilegeEscalation: false`
  - Capabilities: drop ALL
  - Seccomp: RuntimeDefault

#### **Network Access for Worker**

**K8s API Access Enabled:** Yes, via network policy (lines 462-467):
```yaml
allowK8sAPI: true
k8sAPICIDR: "172.16.0.0/28"  # Control plane endpoint CIDR
```

**Network Policy:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/networkpolicy.yaml` (lines 217-245)

The network policy allows egress to K8s API on port 443 to CIDR `172.16.0.0/28` (control plane endpoint).

**Note:** Uses control plane endpoint CIDR instead of ClusterIP because GKE Dataplane V2 (Cilium) evaluates ipBlock rules after DNAT, so ClusterIP (10.20.0.1) won't match.

---

### 4. BuildKit Configuration

**Purpose:** Container image builder for crew workloads (lines 230-281)

**Key Settings:**
- **Service Account:** Uses `crewai-sa`
- **Token Automount:** `true` (line 238)
- **Rootless Mode:** Enabled (runAsUser: 1000)
- **Special Capabilities Required:**
  - `allowPrivilegeEscalation: true` (required for DAC_OVERRIDE in rootless mode)
  - Capabilities: CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE, FOWNER, SETGID, SETUID
  - Seccomp: Unconfined (required for BuildKit)
  - AppArmor: Unconfined

**Artifact Registry Configuration:**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/artifact-registry.tf`

- **Repository:** `crewai` (for crew builds)
- **Access:** Platform SA has `writer` and `reader` roles
- **Purpose:** Stores container images built by BuildKit for crew workloads

---

### 5. Crew Namespace Configuration

#### **Namespace Setup**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/terraform/kubernetes.tf`

**Two Namespaces per Environment:**

1. **Platform Namespace:** `crewai` (lines 24-38)
   - Contains: web, worker, buildkit, replicated, cloud-sql-proxy
   
2. **Crews Namespace:** `crewai-crews` (lines 40-54)
   - Contains: crew workload pods (deployed by platform)

#### **Resource Quotas**

**Platform Namespace (`crewai`)** - lines 135-155:
- CPU: 20 cores request / 40 cores limit
- Memory: 40Gi request / 80Gi limit
- Pods: 50
- Services: 20
- Secrets/ConfigMaps: 30 each

**Crews Namespace (`crewai-crews`)** - lines 157-177:
- CPU: 40 cores request / 80 cores limit
- Memory: 80Gi request / 160Gi limit
- Pods: 100
- Services: 10
- Secrets/ConfigMaps: 20 each

#### **Network Policies**

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/networkpolicy.yaml`

**Key Policies for Crew Communication:**

1. **Allow Crews Namespace Ingress** (lines 88-109):
   - Allows traffic FROM `crewai-crews` namespace TO `crewai` platform pods

2. **Allow Crews Namespace Egress** (lines 350-371):
   - Allows traffic FROM `crewai` platform pods TO `crewai-crews` namespace

**Configuration in values.yaml (lines 483-485):**
```yaml
allowCrewsNamespace: true
crewsNamespace: "crewai-crews"
```

---

### 6. Critical Gaps Identified

#### **Gap 1: Missing RBAC for Crew Provisioning**

**Issue:** The platform service account (`crewai-sa`) has **NO** Role or RoleBinding granting it permissions to create/manage resources in the `crewai-crews` namespace.

**Impact:** Worker pods (which perform "crew provisioning") cannot:
- Create Deployments/Pods in `crewai-crews` namespace
- Create/read ConfigMaps or Secrets
- Manage Jobs or other workload resources
- Access logs or exec into crew pods

**Evidence:**
- No Role/RoleBinding found in Terraform or Helm templates
- Documentation mentions permissions but references non-existent files
- grep for `ClusterRole|ClusterRoleBinding` returned no results

**What's Needed:**
Either:
1. **Role + RoleBinding** in `crewai-crews` namespace (namespace-scoped)
2. **ClusterRole + ClusterRoleBinding** (cluster-wide)

Based on the documentation, a **Role in the `crewai-crews` namespace** is the correct approach.

#### **Gap 2: Undocumented Crew Provisioning Mechanism**

**Issue:** While documentation states workers perform "crew provisioning," there's no clear specification of:
- What Kubernetes API calls are made
- What specific resource types are created
- Whether the upstream CrewAI chart creates its own RBAC

**Implication:** The exact RBAC permissions needed are unclear without examining:
1. The upstream OCI Helm chart (`oci://registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable`)
2. CrewAI application code that interacts with K8s API

---

### 7. Network Security Implementation

#### **Pod Security Standards**

**Environment:** Dev
**PSS Level:** `baseline` mode with `enforce`
**Reason:** BuildKit requires capabilities (CHOWN, DAC_OVERRIDE) blocked by `restricted` PSS

#### **Network Policy Architecture**

**Pattern:** Discrete policies per concern (matches LiteLLM pattern)

**Pod Selectors:**
- CrewAI app pods (web, worker, buildkit): `app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai`
- Replicated SDK pods: `app.kubernetes.io/name: replicated`
- Cloud SQL Proxy: **NOT targeted** by network policies (needs unrestricted private IP egress)

**17 Network Policies Total:**
1. Default deny all ingress (app pods)
2. Allow GCP health checks (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)
3. Allow from Gateway namespace
4. Allow from crews namespace (ingress)
5. Allow OAuth traffic (disabled)
6. Allow DNS egress (CoreDNS + NodeLocal DNSCache)
7. Allow metadata server (Workload Identity)
8. Allow K8s API egress (172.16.0.0/28:443)
9. Allow internet egress (HTTPS, excluding RFC1918)
10. Allow Cloud SQL Proxy egress (in-namespace, port 5432)
11. Allow LiteLLM egress (cross-namespace)
12. Allow crews namespace egress
13-17. Replicated SDK policies (DNS, metadata, K8s API, internet)

---

### 8. ArgoCD Deployment Configuration

**File:** `/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml`

**Multi-Source Configuration:**

1. **Source 1:** CrewAI Platform OCI chart
   - `registry.crewai.com/crewai/stable:crewai-platform`
   - Version: 0.3.13
   - Values from: `$values/environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml`

2. **Source 2:** Local gateway chart
   - `environments/dev/helm/crewai`
   - Contains: network policies, database config, Cloud SQL proxy, jobs

3. **Source 3:** Git reference for values

**Sync Policy:**
- **Automated:** prune + selfHeal enabled
- **Server-Side Apply:** enabled

**Ignore Differences:**
- Deployment replicas (managed by HPA)
- Container resources (adjusted by GKE Autopilot)
- Service annotations (NEG status)
- Secret data (managed by Terraform)

---

### 10. Recommendations for Implementing Crew Provisioning RBAC

Based on the findings, the missing RBAC configuration should include:

1. **Create Role in `crewai-crews` namespace** with permissions for:
   - Pods: create, delete, get, list, watch, patch
   - Pods/exec: create (for debugging)
   - Pods/log: get (for monitoring)
   - Deployments: create, delete, get, list, watch, patch, update
   - Services: create, delete, get, list, watch, patch
   - ConfigMaps: create, delete, get, list, watch, patch
   - Secrets: create, delete, get, list, watch
   - Jobs: create, delete, get, list, watch

2. **Create RoleBinding** linking:
   - Role: (created above)
   - Subject: `serviceAccount:crewai-sa` in namespace `crewai`

3. **Location:** Either as:
   - Terraform resource in `kubernetes.tf`
   - Helm template in local chart
   - Likely best as Terraform to ensure it exists before ArgoCD sync

This would enable the worker pods (using `crewai-sa`) to provision crew workloads in the `crewai-crews` namespace as documented.

    41→  # GCP service account with Cloud SQL, GCS, and Artifact Registry access
    42→  gcpServiceAccount: "crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
    43→
    44→crewNamespace: "crewai-crews"  # Isolated namespace for crew workload execution
    45→
    46→
    47→# =============================================================================
    48→# A2: WEB COMPONENT
    49→# =============================================================================
    50→# Main Rails application serving the CrewAI UI and API.
    51→
    52→web:
    53→  name: "web"
    54→  port: 80
    55→  useHttps: false
    56→  enableSslFromPuma: false
    57→  automountServiceAccountToken: true
    58→  terminationGracePeriodSeconds: 30
    59→
    60→  tls:
    61→    autoGenerate: false
    62→
    63→  resources:
    64→    requests:
    65→      cpu: "500m"
    66→      memory: "1Gi"
    67→    limits:
    68→      cpu: "2000m"
    69→      memory: "4Gi"
    70→
    71→  autoscaling:
    72→    enabled: true
    73→    minReplicas: 1
    74→    maxReplicas: 5
    75→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
    76→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
    77→    behavior:
    78→      scaleUp:
    79→        stabilizationWindowSeconds: 60
    80→        policies:
    81→          - type: Percent
    82→            value: 100
    83→            periodSeconds: 60
    84→      scaleDown:
    85→        stabilizationWindowSeconds: 300
    86→        policies:
    87→          - type: Percent
    88→            value: 25
    89→            periodSeconds: 60
    90→
    91→  livenessProbe:
    92→    httpGet:
    93→      path: /health
    94→      port: 80
    95→      scheme: HTTP
    96→    initialDelaySeconds: 60
    97→    periodSeconds: 10
    98→    timeoutSeconds: 5
    99→    failureThreshold: 3
   100→
   101→  readinessProbe:
   102→    httpGet:
   103→      path: /health
   104→      port: 80
   105→      scheme: HTTP
   106→    initialDelaySeconds: 30
   107→    periodSeconds: 5
   108→    timeoutSeconds: 3
   109→    failureThreshold: 3
   110→
   111→  startupProbe:
   112→    httpGet:
   113→      path: /health
   114→      port: 80
   115→      scheme: HTTP
   116→    initialDelaySeconds: 0
   117→    periodSeconds: 10
   118→    timeoutSeconds: 5
   119→    failureThreshold: 30
   120→
   121→  podSecurityContext:
   122→    runAsNonRoot: true
   123→    runAsUser: 1000
   124→    runAsGroup: 1000
   125→    fsGroup: 1000
   126→    fsGroupChangePolicy: "OnRootMismatch"
   127→    seccompProfile:
   128→      type: RuntimeDefault
   129→
   130→  securityContext:
   131→    runAsNonRoot: true
   132→    runAsUser: 1000
   133→    allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
   134→    capabilities:
   135→      drop:
   136→        - ALL
   137→    seccompProfile:
   138→      type: RuntimeDefault
   139→
   140→  service:
   141→    type: ClusterIP
   142→    annotations:
   143→      cloud.google.com/neg: '{"ingress":true,"exposed_ports":{"80":{}}}'
   144→
   145→  ingress:
   146→    enabled: false  # Using Gateway API
   147→
   148→  podDisruptionBudget:
   149→    enabled: true
   150→    minAvailable: 1
   151→
   152→
   153→# =============================================================================
   154→# A3: WORKER COMPONENT
   155→# =============================================================================
   156→# Background job processor (Sidekiq) for async tasks and crew provisioning.
   157→
   158→worker:
   159→  automountServiceAccountToken: true
   160→
   161→  resources:
   162→    requests:
   163→      cpu: "500m"
   164→      memory: "1Gi"
   165→    limits:
   166→      cpu: "2000m"
   167→      memory: "4Gi"
   168→
   169→  autoscaling:
   170→    enabled: true
   171→    minReplicas: 1
   172→    maxReplicas: 5
   173→    targetCPUUtilizationPercentage: 70
   174→    targetMemoryUtilizationPercentage: 80
   175→    behavior:
   176→      scaleUp:
   177→        stabilizationWindowSeconds: 60
   178→        policies:
   179→          - type: Percent
   180→            value: 100
   181→            periodSeconds: 60
   182→      scaleDown:
   183→        stabilizationWindowSeconds: 300
   184→        policies:
   185→          - type: Percent
   186→            value: 25
   187→            periodSeconds: 60
   188→
   189→  livenessProbe:
   190→    exec:
   191→      command: ["/bin/sh", "-c", "pgrep -f worker"]
   192→    initialDelaySeconds: 60
   193→    periodSeconds: 30
   194→    timeoutSeconds: 5
   195→    failureThreshold: 3
   196→
   197→  readinessProbe:
   198→    exec:
   199→      command: ["/bin/sh", "-c", "pgrep -f worker"]
   200→    initialDelaySeconds: 30
   201→    periodSeconds: 10
   202→    timeoutSeconds: 3
   203→    failureThreshold: 3
   204→
   205→  podSecurityContext:
   206→    runAsNonRoot: true
   207→    runAsUser: 1000
   208→    runAsGroup: 1000
   209→    fsGroup: 1000
   210→    fsGroupChangePolicy: "OnRootMismatch"
   211→    seccompProfile:
   212→      type: RuntimeDefault
   213→
   214→  securityContext:
   215→    runAsNonRoot: true
   216→    runAsUser: 1000
   217→    allowPrivilegeEscalation: false
   218→    capabilities:
   219→      drop:
   220→        - ALL
   221→    seccompProfile:
   222→      type: RuntimeDefault
   223→
   224→  podDisruptionBudget:
   225→    enabled: true
   226→    minAvailable: 1
   227→
   228→
   229→# =============================================================================
   230→# A4: BUILDKIT COMPONENT
   231→# =============================================================================
   232→# Container image builder for crew workloads.
   233→# Single replica - builds are queued, not parallelized.
   234→
   235→buildkit:
   236→  enabled: true
   237→  replicaCount: 1
   238→  automountServiceAccountToken: true
   239→
   240→  rootless:
   241→    enabled: true
   242→    runAsUser: 1000
   243→    runAsGroup: 1000
   244→    fsGroup: 1000
   245→
   246→  resources:
   247→    requests:
   248→      cpu: "250m"
   249→      memory: "1Gi"
   250→    limits:
   251→      cpu: "2000m"
   252→      memory: "4Gi"
   253→
   254→  podSecurityContext:
   255→    runAsNonRoot: true
   256→    runAsUser: 1000
   257→    runAsGroup: 1000
   258→    fsGroup: 1000
   259→    fsGroupChangePolicy: "OnRootMismatch"
   260→    seccompProfile:
   261→      type: Unconfined
   262→
   263→  securityContext:
   264→    runAsNonRoot: true
   265→    runAsUser: 1000
   266→    runAsGroup: 1000
   267→    allowPrivilegeEscalation: true  # Required for DAC_OVERRIDE capability in rootless BuildKit
   268→    capabilities:
   269→      drop:
   270→        - ALL
   271→      add:
   272→        - CHOWN
   273→        - DAC_OVERRIDE
   274→        - FOWNER
   275→        - SETGID
   276→        - SETUID
   277→    seccompProfile:
   278→      type: Unconfined
   279→    appArmorProfile:
   280→      type: Unconfined
   281→
   282→
   283→# =============================================================================
   284→# A5: REPLICATED SDK
   285→# =============================================================================
   286→# License validation and update checking for CrewAI Enterprise.
   287→
   288→replicated:
   289→  isAirgap: false
   290→  replicatedID: "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
   291→  image:
   292→    repository: "crewai-images/replicated-sdk-image"
   293→  extraEnv:
   294→    - name: AUTH_TOKEN
   295→      valueFrom:
   296→        secretKeyRef:
   297→          name: crewai-secrets
   298→          key: REPLICATED_AUTH_TOKEN
   299→
   300→
   301→# =============================================================================
   302→# A6: ENVIRONMENT VARIABLES
   303→# =============================================================================
   304→# Application configuration. Secrets injected via envFrom.
   305→
   306→envVars:
   307→  # Application
   308→  APPLICATION_HOST: "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
   309→  RAILS_ENV: "production"
   310→  CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: "europe-west1-docker.pkg.dev/kd-ix-eur-dev-gke/crewai"
   311→
   312→  # Database (Cloud SQL via proxy with IAM auth)
   313→  DB_HOST: "cloud-sql-proxy"
   314→  DB_PORT: "5432"
   315→  DB_USER: "crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam"
   316→  POSTGRES_DB: "crewai_plus_production"
   317→  POSTGRES_CABLE_DB: "crewai_plus_cable_production"
   318→  POSTGRES_OAUTH_DB: "crewai_plus_oauth_db"
   319→
   320→  # Storage (GCS with S3-compatible API)
   321→  STORAGE_SERVICE: "amazon"
   322→  AWS_REGION: "auto"
   323→  AWS_BUCKET: "crewai-data-dev"
   324→  AWS_ENDPOINT: "https://storage.googleapis.com"
   325→
   326→  # Authentication (WorkOS)
   327→  AUTH_PROVIDER: "local"
   328→  WORKOS_REDIRECT_URI: "https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com/auth/workos/callback"
   329→  WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN: "undisputed-tip-18-staging.authkit.app"
   330→  # Secrets: WORKOS_CLIENT_ID, WORKOS_API_KEY, WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD → crewai-secrets
   331→
   332→  # GitHub App (repository integration)
   333→  GITHUB_CLIENT_ID: "Iv23liOX8MzQ4WERs6LQ"
   334→  GITHUB_CALLBACK_URL: "https://crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com/crewai_plus/organizations/github/callback"
   335→  GITHUB_APP_URL: "https://github.com/apps/crewai-git-dev/installations/new"
   336→  GITHUB_APP_ID: "2850358"
   337→  # Secrets: GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET, GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY → crewai-secrets
   338→
   339→envFrom:
   340→  - secretRef:
   341→      name: "crewai-secrets"
   342→
   343→
   344→# =============================================================================
   345→# A7: DISABLED UPSTREAM FEATURES
   346→# =============================================================================
   347→# Using external managed services instead of in-cluster deployments.
   348→
   349→postgres:
   350→  enabled: false  # Using Cloud SQL
   351→
   352→minio:
   353→  enabled: false  # Using GCS
   354→
   355→
   356→# #############################################################################
   357→#
   358→#                    PART B: LOCAL GATEWAY CHART
   359→#
   360→# #############################################################################
   361→# Values consumed by our local Helm chart templates.
   362→# Path: environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/
   363→# =============================================================================
   364→
   365→
   366→# =============================================================================
   367→# B1: GATEWAY API CONFIGURATION
   368→# =============================================================================
   369→# External routing via GKE Gateway API (L7 load balancer).
   370→
   371→hostname: "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
   372→
   373→gateway:
   374→  name: "crewai-gateway"
   375→  certificateMap: "kd-ix-eur-dev-cert-crewai-map"
   376→
   377→httpRoute:
   378→  hostnames:
   379→    - "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
   380→
   381→httpRedirectRoute:
   382→  hostnames:
   383→    - "crewai.dev.ix.konecta-digital.com"
   384→
   385→# Backend service reference (for HTTPRoute)
   386→crewai:
   387→  namespace: "crewai"
   388→  serviceName: "crewai-web"
   389→  servicePort: 80
   390→
   391→# Dev: full request logging for debugging
   392→gcpBackendPolicy:
   393→  logging:
   394→    enabled: true
   395→    sampleRate: 1.0
   396→
   397→healthCheckPolicy:
   398→  default:
   399→    logConfig:
   400→      enabled: true
   401→
   402→
   403→# =============================================================================
   404→# B2: CLOUD SQL PROXY
   405→# =============================================================================
   406→# Secure database connectivity via Workload Identity.
   407→
   408→cloudSqlProxy:
   409→  enabled: true
   410→  connectionName: "kd-ix-eur-dev-platform-data:europe-west1:kd-ix-eur-dev-cloudsql-platform"
   411→  replicas: 1
   412→  image: "gcr.io/cloud-sql-connectors/cloud-sql-proxy:2.8.2"
   413→  resources:
   414→    requests:
   415→      cpu: "100m"
   416→      memory: "128Mi"
   417→    limits:
   418→      cpu: "500m"
   419→      memory: "512Mi"
   420→
   421→
   422→# =============================================================================
   423→# B3: DATABASE JOBS
   424→# =============================================================================
   425→# PreSync jobs for database setup before application deployment.
   426→
   427→# PreSync ConfigMap for Rails database.yml
   428→databaseConfig:
   429→  enabled: true
   430→
   431→# PreSync job to grant schema permissions to IAM user before migrations
   432→databasePermissions:
   433→  iamUser: "crewai-platform@kd-ix-eur-dev-gke.iam"
   434→  postgresSecretName: "postgres-credentials"
   435→  postgresSecretKey: "password"
   436→  postgresSecretGcpName: "cloudsql-postgres-password"
   437→
   438→
   439→# =============================================================================
   440→# B4: NETWORK POLICIES
   441→# =============================================================================
   442→# Pod-level network security for GKE Dataplane V2.
   443→# Discrete policies per concern (matches LiteLLM pattern).
   444→# crewai app pods (web, worker, buildkit) → app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   445→# replicated SDK pods → app.kubernetes.io/name: replicated
   446→# cloud-sql-proxy is NOT targeted (needs unrestricted private IP egress)
   447→
   448→networkPolicies:
   449→  enabled: true
   450→
   451→  # GCP health check IP ranges (CRITICAL for load balancer backends)
   452→  allowHealthCheckIPs:
   453→    - 35.191.0.0/16
   454→    - 130.211.0.0/22
   455→
   456→  # Allow DNS resolution (CoreDNS + NodeLocal DNSCache)
   457→  allowDNS: true
   458→
   459→  # Allow GKE metadata server (Workload Identity)
   460→  allowMetadata: true
   461→
   462→  # Allow Kubernetes API server
   463→  # NOTE: Use control plane endpoint CIDR (172.16.0.0/28), NOT ClusterIP (10.20.0.1).
   464→  # GKE Dataplane V2 (Cilium) evaluates ipBlock after DNAT, so ClusterIP won't match.
   465→  allowK8sAPI: true
   466→  k8sAPICIDR: "172.16.0.0/28"
   467→
   468→  # Allow external HTTPS egress (GCS, WorkOS, external APIs)
   469→  allowInternetEgress: true
   470→
   471→  # Allow Cloud SQL Proxy (in-namespace, port 5432)
   472→  allowCloudSQLProxy: true
   473→
   474→  # Allow LiteLLM (cross-namespace, AI inference)
   475→  allowLiteLLM: true
   476→  litellmNamespace: "litellm"
   477→  litellmPorts:
   478→    - protocol: TCP
   479→      port: 80   # service port
   480→    - protocol: TCP
   481→      port: 4000  # pod port (Dataplane v2)
   482→
   483→  # Allow crewai-crews namespace (crew provisioning + workload comms)
   484→  allowCrewsNamespace: true
   485→  crewsNamespace: "crewai-crews"
   486→
   487→  # Replicated SDK policies (license validation, update checks)
   488→  replicated:
   489→    enabled: true
   490→
   491→
   492→# =============================================================================
   493→# B5: DISABLED LOCAL FEATURES
   494→# =============================================================================
   495→
   496→oauth:
   497→  enabled: false  # Using WorkOS
   498→
   499→cloudArmor:
   500→  enabled: false  # Not required for dev
   501→
   502→podDisruptionBudget:
   503→  enabled: false  # Single replica in dev; upstream chart manages PDBs for web/worker
   504→
   505→# DISABLED: Moved to GitHub Actions (manual-org-initial-setup.yml)
   506→postInstallSetup:
   507→  enabled: false
   508→  serviceAccount: "post-install-setup-sa"
   509→  ownerOrgId: "2"
   510→  ownerEmail: "kix-platform@konecta.com"
   511→
   512→
   513→# =============================================================================
   514→# REFERENCE: Sync Wave Order
   515→# =============================================================================
   516→# PreSync:
   517→#   -35  serviceaccount (crewai-sa with Workload Identity)
   518→#   -30  cloud-sql-proxy (deployment + service)
   519→#   -25  externalsecret-postgres
   520→#   -20  job-db-permissions
   521→#   -15  configmap-database
   522→#     0  main application (web, worker, buildkit)
   523→#
   524→# PostSync:
   525→#    +5  rbac-post-install-setup
   526→#   +10  job-post-install-setup
   527→# =============================================================================
   528→
```

     1→{{- /*
     2→Service Account for CrewAI platform workloads.
     3→PreSync at wave -35 ensures it exists before cloud-sql-proxy (-30).
     4→The Workload Identity annotation links to the GCP SA created by Terraform.
     5→Terraform's kubernetes_service_account uses lifecycle ignore to prevent drift.
     6→*/ -}}
     7→---
     8→apiVersion: v1
     9→kind: ServiceAccount
    10→metadata:
    11→  name: {{ .Values.serviceAccount }}
    12→  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
    13→  labels:
    14→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    15→    app.kubernetes.io/component: platform
    16→  annotations:
    17→    argocd.argoproj.io/hook: PreSync
    18→    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: "-35"
    19→    # Workload Identity - matches GCP SA created by Terraform
    20→    iam.gke.io/gcp-service-account: {{ .Values.workloadIdentity.gcpServiceAccount }}
    21→

```
     1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     2→# Kubernetes Resources
     3→#
     4→# Namespaces, service accounts, limit ranges, and resource quotas for CrewAI.
     5→# Terraform creates the namespaces so K8s resources can be provisioned.
     6→# ArgoCD will adopt the namespace via CreateNamespace=true (no-op if exists).
     7→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     8→
     9→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    10→# Local Variables for Namespace Names
    11→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    12→
    13→locals {
    14→  platform_namespace = "crewai"
    15→  crews_namespace    = "crewai-crews"
    16→}
    17→
    18→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    19→# Namespaces
    20→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    21→# Created by Terraform to ensure K8s resources can be provisioned.
    22→# ArgoCD will adopt these namespaces (CreateNamespace is a no-op if exists).
    23→
    24→resource "kubernetes_namespace" "crewai_platform" {
    25→  metadata {
    26→    name = local.platform_namespace
    27→    labels = merge(local.labels, {
    28→      "app.kubernetes.io/managed-by" = "terraform"
    29→    })
    30→  }
    31→
    32→  lifecycle {
    33→    ignore_changes = [
    34→      metadata[0].labels,
    35→      metadata[0].annotations,
    36→    ]
    37→  }
    38→}
    39→
    40→resource "kubernetes_namespace" "crewai_crews" {
    41→  metadata {
    42→    name = local.crews_namespace
    43→    labels = merge(local.labels, {
    44→      "app.kubernetes.io/managed-by" = "terraform"
    45→    })
    46→  }
    47→
    48→  lifecycle {
    49→    ignore_changes = [
    50→      metadata[0].labels,
    51→      metadata[0].annotations,
    52→    ]
    53→  }
    54→}
    55→
    56→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    57→# Kubernetes Service Accounts
    58→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    59→# Platform SA is created by Helm (PreSync wave -35) to ensure it exists
    60→# before cloud-sql-proxy deployment. Terraform manages the GCP SA and IAM
    61→# bindings; the Workload Identity binding references the K8s SA by name.
    62→#
    63→# Crews SA is created here - crew pods don't need Helm sync wave ordering.
    64→# Token automount is disabled for security; pods explicitly mount if needed.
    65→
    66→resource "kubernetes_service_account" "crewai_crews" {
    67→  metadata {
    68→    name      = "crewai-crews-sa"
    69→    namespace = local.crews_namespace
    70→    labels    = local.labels
    71→  }
    72→  automount_service_account_token = false
    73→
    74→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_crews]
    75→}
    76→
    77→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    78→# Limit Ranges
    79→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    80→# Default resource limits for containers without explicit limits set.
    81→# Platform namespace has lower defaults; crews namespace allows more resources.
    82→
    83→resource "kubernetes_limit_range" "crewai_platform" {
    84→  metadata {
    85→    name      = "crewai-platform-limits"
    86→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
    87→    labels    = local.labels
    88→  }
    89→  spec {
    90→    limit {
    91→      type = "Container"
    92→      default = {
    93→        cpu    = "500m"
    94→        memory = "512Mi"
    95→      }
    96→      default_request = {
    97→        cpu    = "100m"
    98→        memory = "128Mi"
    99→      }
   100→    }
   101→  }
   102→
   103→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform]
   104→}
   105→
   106→resource "kubernetes_limit_range" "crewai_crews" {
   107→  metadata {
   108→    name      = "crewai-crews-limits"
   109→    namespace = local.crews_namespace
   110→    labels    = local.labels
   111→  }
   112→  spec {
   113→    limit {
   114→      type = "Container"
   115→      default = {
   116→        cpu    = "1"
   117→        memory = "1Gi"
   118→      }
   119→      default_request = {
   120→        cpu    = "250m"
   121→        memory = "256Mi"
   122→      }
   123→    }
   124→  }
   125→
   126→  depends_on = [kubernetes_namespace.crewai_crews]
   127→}
   128→
   129→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   130→# Resource Quotas
   131→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   132→# Hard limits on total resources consumed per namespace.
   133→# Platform namespace is smaller; crews namespace allows burst capacity.
   134→
   135→resource "kubernetes_resource_quota" "crewai_platform" {
   136→  metadata {
   137→    name      = "crewai-platform-quota"
   138→    namespace = local.platform_namespace
   139→    labels    = local.labels
   140→  }
   141→  spec {
   142→    hard = {
   143→      "requests.cpu"         = "20"
   144→      "requests.memory"      = "40Gi"
   145→      "limits.cpu"           = "40"
   146→      "limits.memory"        = "80Gi"
   147→      pods                   = "50"
   148→      services               = "20"
   149→      secrets                = "30"
   150→      configmaps             = "30"
   151→      persistentvolumeclaims = "10"
   152→    }
   153→  }
   154→  depends_on = [kubernetes_limit_range.crewai_platform]
   155→}
   156→
   157→resource "kubernetes_resource_quota" "crewai_crews" {
   158→  metadata {
   159→    name      = "crewai-crews-quota"
   160→    namespace = local.crews_namespace
   161→    labels    = local.labels
   162→  }
   163→  spec {
   164→    hard = {
   165→      "requests.cpu"         = "40"
   166→      "requests.memory"      = "80Gi"
   167→      "limits.cpu"           = "80"
   168→      "limits.memory"        = "160Gi"
   169→      pods                   = "100"
   170→      services               = "10"
   171→      secrets                = "20"
   172→      configmaps             = "20"
   173→      persistentvolumeclaims = "10"
   174→    }
   175→  }
   176→  depends_on = [kubernetes_limit_range.crewai_crews]
   177→}
   178→
```

     1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     2→# Service Accounts and IAM Bindings
     3→#
     4→# GCP service account for CrewAI platform workloads with Workload Identity.
     5→# Includes all IAM bindings for Cloud SQL, GCS, and Artifact Registry access.
     6→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     7→
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→# GCP Service Account
    10→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    11→# Created inline (not via module) to avoid circular dependencies with HMAC keys
    12→# and secrets that reference the service account email.
    13→
    14→resource "google_service_account" "crewai_platform" {
    15→  project      = var.gke_project_id
    16→  account_id   = "crewai-platform"
    17→  display_name = "CrewAI Platform"
    18→}
    19→
    20→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    21→# IAM: Cloud SQL Access
    22→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    23→# Allows the service account to connect to Cloud SQL via the Cloud SQL Proxy.
    24→
    25→resource "google_project_iam_member" "cloudsql_client" {
    26→  project = var.platform_data_project
    27→  role    = "roles/cloudsql.client"
    28→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    29→}
    30→
    31→resource "google_project_iam_member" "cloudsql_instance_user" {
    32→  project = var.platform_data_project
    33→  role    = "roles/cloudsql.instanceUser"
    34→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    35→}
    36→
    37→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    38→# IAM: GCS Bucket Access
    39→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    40→# Data bucket: full object admin for reading and writing application data.
    41→# Logs bucket: object creator only for write-only audit logging.
    42→
    43→resource "google_storage_bucket_iam_member" "data_bucket" {
    44→  bucket = module.gcs_data.bucket_name
    45→  role   = "roles/storage.objectAdmin"
    46→  member = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    47→}
    48→
    49→resource "google_storage_bucket_iam_member" "logs_bucket" {
    50→  bucket = module.gcs_logs.bucket_name
    51→  role   = "roles/storage.objectCreator"
    52→  member = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    53→}
    54→
    55→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    56→# IAM: Shared Artifact Registry Access
    57→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    58→# Allows the service account to pull container images from the shared
    59→# Artifact Registry repository (crewai-images in kd-ix-eur-shr-artifacts).
    60→# These are the mirrored CrewAI platform images synced from images.crewai.com.
    61→
    62→resource "google_artifact_registry_repository_iam_member" "shared_ar_reader" {
    63→  project    = var.shared_artifacts_project
    64→  location   = var.region
    65→  repository = var.shared_ar_repository
    66→  role       = "roles/artifactregistry.reader"
    67→  member     = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    68→}
    69→
    70→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    71→# IAM: Secret Manager Access (for External Secrets Operator)
    72→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    73→# Allows the service account to access secrets in Secret Manager.
    74→# Required for ESO to pull secrets and create Kubernetes secrets.
    75→
    76→resource "google_project_iam_member" "secret_accessor" {
    77→  project = var.security_project_id
    78→  role    = "roles/secretmanager.secretAccessor"
    79→  member  = "serviceAccount:${google_service_account.crewai_platform.email}"
    80→}
    81→
    82→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    83→# IAM: Workload Identity Binding
    84→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    85→# Allows the Kubernetes service account to impersonate the GCP service account.
    86→# This enables pods to authenticate to GCP services without service account keys.
    87→
    88→resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "workload_identity" {
    89→  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name
    90→  role               = "roles/iam.workloadIdentityUser"
    91→  member             = "serviceAccount:${var.gke_project_id}.svc.id.goog[crewai/crewai-sa]"
    92→}
    93→
    94→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    95→# IAM: GitHub Actions Impersonation
    96→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    97→# Allows GitHub Actions to impersonate this service account for CI/CD.
    98→# The GitHub service account is created separately in the platform infrastructure.
    99→
   100→resource "google_service_account_iam_member" "github_impersonation" {
   101→  service_account_id = google_service_account.crewai_platform.name
   102→  role               = "roles/iam.serviceAccountTokenCreator"
   103→  member             = "serviceAccount:github-crewai-${var.environment}@${var.gke_project_id}.iam.gserviceaccount.com"
   104→}
   105→
```

```
     1→# CrewAI Crew Namespace Configuration
     2→
     3→## Overview
     4→
     5→The CrewAI platform uses **separate namespaces** for platform components and deployed crew workloads:
     6→
     7→- **Platform Namespace** (`crewai-{env}`): Contains the platform components (web, worker, buildkit)
     8→- **Crew Namespace** (`crewai-crews-{env}`): Contains deployed crew workloads
     9→
    10→This separation provides:
    11→- **Resource isolation**: Crews run in their own namespace with dedicated quotas
    12→- **Security boundaries**: Network policies control cross-namespace communication
    13→- **RBAC granularity**: Platform service account has elevated permissions in crew namespace
    14→- **Operational clarity**: Easier monitoring, debugging, and management of crew workloads
    15→
    16→## Configuration
    17→
    18→### Helm Values
    19→
    20→Each environment's Helm values file (`helm/values/values-{env}.yaml`) includes:
    21→
    22→```yaml
    23→# Crew workloads namespace (separate from platform namespace)
    24→# This is where deployed crews will execute
    25→crewNamespace: "crewai-crews-{env}"
    26→```
    27→
    28→### Environment-Specific Namespaces
    29→
    30→| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace         |
    31→|-------------|-------------------|------------------------|
    32→| Dev         | `crewai-dev`      | `crewai-crews-dev`    |
    33→| QA          | `crewai-qa`       | `crewai-crews-qa`     |
    34→| Prod        | `crewai-prod`     | `crewai-crews-prod`   |
    35→
    36→## Architecture Components
    37→
    38→### 1. Namespace Manifests
    39→
    40→**Base Configuration:**
    41→- `kubernetes/kustomize/base/namespace/namespace.yaml` - Platform namespace
    42→- `kubernetes/kustomize/base/namespace/crew-namespace.yaml` - Crew namespace
    43→
    44→**Labels:**
    45→- Platform namespace: `component: platform`
    46→- Crew namespace: `component: crews`
    47→
    48→### 2. RBAC Configuration
    49→
    50→**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/rbac/crew-access-role.yaml`
    51→
    52→The platform service account (`crewai-platform-sa`) has:
    53→- **In platform namespace**: Limited permissions (ConfigMaps, Secrets read-only)
    54→- **In crew namespace**: Full management permissions for crew workloads
    55→
    56→**Granted Permissions in Crew Namespace:**
    57→- Full Pod management (create, delete, exec, logs)
    58→- ConfigMap and Secret management
    59→- Deployment, StatefulSet, Job management
    60→- Service and PVC management
    61→
    62→### 3. Network Policies
    63→
    64→**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/network-policies/allow-crews.yaml`
    65→
    66→**Default Deny:** All traffic is denied by default in crew namespace
    67→
    68→**Allowed Ingress (to crews):**
    69→- From platform web pods → crew pods (port 8080)
    70→- From platform worker pods → crew pods (port 8080)
    71→
    72→**Allowed Egress (from crews):**
    73→- To DNS (kube-system namespace)
    74→- To GCP metadata server (169.254.169.254) - for Workload Identity
    75→- To external HTTPS (GCP APIs, external services)
    76→- To platform buildkit (for container builds)
    77→- To platform worker (for job management)
    78→- To platform web (for API callbacks)
    79→
    80→### 4. Resource Quotas
    81→
    82→**Location:** `kubernetes/kustomize/base/resource-limits/crew-resource-quota.yaml`
    83→
    84→#### Development/QA (Default)
    85→```yaml
    86→CPU: 20 cores request / 40 cores limit
    87→Memory: 40Gi request / 80Gi limit
    88→Pods: 100
    89→Storage: 200Gi
    90→```
    91→
    92→#### Production (Scaled)
    93→```yaml
    94→CPU: 80 cores request / 160 cores limit
    95→Memory: 160Gi request / 320Gi limit
    96→Pods: 300
    97→Storage: 1Ti
    98→```
    99→
   100→**Per-Container Limits:**
   101→```yaml
   102→Max: 4 CPU / 8Gi memory
   103→Default: 500m CPU / 1Gi memory
   104→Default Request: 250m CPU / 512Mi memory
   105→```
   106→
   107→## Pod Security Standards
   108→
   109→Both namespaces enforce Pod Security Admission policies:
   110→
   111→| Environment | Platform Namespace | Crew Namespace    |
   112→|-------------|-------------------|-------------------|
   113→| Dev         | Baseline          | Baseline          |
   114→| QA          | Baseline/Audit Restricted | Baseline/Audit Restricted |
   115→| Prod        | Restricted        | Restricted        |
   116→
   117→## Deployment Workflow
   118→
   119→### 1. Namespace Creation
   120→
   121→Namespaces are created via Kustomize overlays during ArgoCD sync:
   122→
   123→```bash
   124→# Created in sync-wave -5 (very early)
   125→kubectl get namespaces -l platform=crewai
   126→```
   127→
   128→### 2. RBAC Setup
   129→
   130→Platform service account binding to crew namespace happens in sync-wave -1:
   131→
   132→```bash
   133→# Verify RBAC
   134→kubectl get rolebinding -n crewai-crews-dev crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding
   135→```
   136→
   137→### 3. Network Policy Application
   138→
   139→Network policies are applied in sync-wave 0:
   140→
   141→```bash
   142→# Verify network policies
   143→kubectl get networkpolicies -n crewai-crews-dev
   144→```
   145→
   146→### 4. Crew Deployment
   147→
   148→Platform components can now deploy crews to the crew namespace:
   149→
   150→```bash
   151→# Example: Platform creates a crew deployment
   152→kubectl create deployment my-crew \
   153→  --image=registry.crewai.com/crew:latest \
   154→  --namespace=crewai-crews-dev
   155→```
   156→
   157→## Monitoring and Operations
   158→
   159→### Check Crew Namespace Status
   160→
   161→```bash
   162→# List all resources in crew namespace
   163→kubectl get all -n crewai-crews-dev
   164→
   165→# Check resource quota usage
   166→kubectl describe resourcequota crew-quota -n crewai-crews-dev
   167→
   168→# View network policies
   169→kubectl describe networkpolicy -n crewai-crews-dev
   170→```
   171→
   172→### Debug Crew Connectivity
   173→
   174→```bash
   175→# Test DNS resolution from a crew pod
   176→kubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- nslookup crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local
   177→
   178→# Check if crew can reach platform web
   179→kubectl exec -it <crew-pod> -n crewai-crews-dev -- curl -v http://crewai-web.crewai-dev.svc.cluster.local:443
   180→```
   181→
   182→### View RBAC Permissions
   183→
   184→```bash
   185→# Check what platform SA can do in crew namespace
   186→kubectl auth can-i --list --as=system:serviceaccount:crewai-dev:crewai-platform-sa -n crewai-crews-dev
   187→```
   188→
   189→## Troubleshooting
   190→
   191→### Issue: Crew pods cannot reach platform services
   192→
   193→**Cause:** Network policy blocking traffic
   194→
   195→**Solution:**
   196→1. Verify crew pods have correct labels: `platform: crewai`, `component: crews`
   197→2. Check network policy allows egress to platform namespace:
   198→   ```bash
   199→   kubectl describe networkpolicy allow-crew-egress -n crewai-crews-dev
   200→   ```
   201→
   202→### Issue: Platform cannot create crews
   203→
   204→**Cause:** Missing RBAC permissions
   205→
   206→**Solution:**
   207→1. Verify RoleBinding exists:
   208→   ```bash
   209→   kubectl get rolebinding crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding -n crewai-crews-dev
   210→   ```
   211→2. Check service account is correct:
   212→   ```bash
   213→   kubectl get rolebinding crewai-platform-crew-manager-binding -n crewai-crews-dev -o yaml
   214→   ```
   215→
   216→### Issue: Resource quota exceeded
   217→
   218→**Cause:** Too many crews running
   219→
   220→**Solution:**
   221→1. Check current usage:
   222→   ```bash
   223→   kubectl describe resourcequota crew-quota -n crewai-crews-dev
   224→   ```
   225→2. Scale down or delete unused crews:
   226→   ```bash
   227→   kubectl get deployments -n crewai-crews-dev
   228→   kubectl delete deployment <unused-crew> -n crewai-crews-dev
   229→   ```
   230→
   231→## Security Considerations
   232→
   233→1. **Namespace Isolation**: Crews cannot access platform namespace resources directly
   234→2. **Network Segmentation**: Default deny policy prevents unauthorized communication
   235→3. **Resource Limits**: Prevents resource exhaustion attacks
   236→4. **RBAC Separation**: Platform components have minimal permissions in their own namespace, elevated only in crew namespace
   237→5. **Pod Security**: Enforces baseline/restricted standards to prevent privilege escalation
   238→
   239→## References
   240→
   241→- [CrewAI Configuration Documentation](https://enterprise-docs.crewai.com/reference/chart-values#param-crew-namespace)
   242→- [Kubernetes RBAC](https://kubernetes.io/docs/reference/access-authn-authz/rbac/)
   243→- [Kubernetes Network Policies](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/services-networking/network-policies/)
   244→- [Pod Security Standards](https://kubernetes.io/docs/concepts/security/pod-security-standards/)
   245→
```

```
     1→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.enabled }}
     2→---
     3→# =============================================================================
     4→# Network Policy 1: Default Deny All Ingress
     5→# Purpose: Deny all inbound traffic by default (security baseline)
     6→# =============================================================================
     7→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
     8→kind: NetworkPolicy
     9→metadata:
    10→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-deny-all-ingress
    11→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
    12→  labels:
    13→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    14→  annotations:
    15→    description: "Default deny all ingress to CrewAI application pods"
    16→spec:
    17→  podSelector:
    18→    matchLabels:
    19→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
    20→  policyTypes:
    21→    - Ingress
    22→
    23→---
    24→# =============================================================================
    25→# Network Policy 2: Allow GCP Health Checks
    26→# Purpose: CRITICAL - Allow GCP load balancer health checks
    27→# Without this, load balancer will mark backends as unhealthy
    28→# =============================================================================
    29→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
    30→kind: NetworkPolicy
    31→metadata:
    32→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-health-checks
    33→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
    34→  labels:
    35→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    36→  annotations:
    37→    description: "Allow GCP health check IP ranges (35.191.0.0/16, 130.211.0.0/22)"
    38→spec:
    39→  podSelector:
    40→    matchLabels:
    41→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
    42→  policyTypes:
    43→    - Ingress
    44→  ingress:
    45→    {{- range .Values.networkPolicies.allowHealthCheckIPs }}
    46→    - from:
    47→        - ipBlock:
    48→            cidr: {{ . }}
    49→      ports:
    50→        - protocol: TCP
    51→          port: {{ $.Values.crewai.servicePort }}
    52→    {{- end }}
    53→
    54→---
    55→# =============================================================================
    56→# Network Policy 3: Allow from Gateway Namespace
    57→# Purpose: Allow traffic from GKE Gateway controller for routing
    58→# =============================================================================
    59→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
    60→kind: NetworkPolicy
    61→metadata:
    62→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-gateway
    63→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
    64→  labels:
    65→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    66→  annotations:
    67→    description: "Allow ingress from GKE Gateway controller namespace"
    68→spec:
    69→  podSelector:
    70→    matchLabels:
    71→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
    72→  policyTypes:
    73→    - Ingress
    74→  ingress:
    75→    - from:
    76→        - namespaceSelector:
    77→            matchLabels:
    78→              kubernetes.io/metadata.name: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
    79→      ports:
    80→        - protocol: TCP
    81→          port: {{ .Values.crewai.servicePort }}
    82→
    83→---
    84→# =============================================================================
    85→# Network Policy 4: Allow from Crew Workloads Namespace
    86→# Purpose: Allow crew workload pods to communicate back to platform
    87→# =============================================================================
    88→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowCrewsNamespace }}
    89→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
    90→kind: NetworkPolicy
    91→metadata:
    92→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-crews-ingress
    93→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
    94→  labels:
    95→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
    96→  annotations:
    97→    description: "Allow ingress from crewai-crews namespace"
    98→spec:
    99→  podSelector:
   100→    matchLabels:
   101→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   102→  policyTypes:
   103→    - Ingress
   104→  ingress:
   105→    - from:
   106→        - namespaceSelector:
   107→            matchLabels:
   108→              kubernetes.io/metadata.name: {{ .Values.networkPolicies.crewsNamespace }}
   109→{{- end }}
   110→
   111→{{- if .Values.oauth.enabled }}
   112→---
   113→# =============================================================================
   114→# Network Policy 5: Allow OAuth Traffic
   115→# Purpose: Allow traffic to OAuth service
   116→# =============================================================================
   117→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   118→kind: NetworkPolicy
   119→metadata:
   120→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-oauth
   121→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
   122→  labels:
   123→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
   124→  annotations:
   125→    description: "Allow ingress to OAuth service"
   126→spec:
   127→  podSelector:
   128→    matchLabels:
   129→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   130→  policyTypes:
   131→    - Ingress
   132→  ingress:
   133→    - from:
   134→        - namespaceSelector:
   135→            matchLabels:
   136→              kubernetes.io/metadata.name: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
   137→      ports:
   138→        - protocol: TCP
   139→          port: {{ .Values.oauth.servicePort }}
   140→{{- end }}
   141→
   142→---
   143→# =============================================================================
   144→# Network Policy 6: Allow DNS Egress
   145→# Purpose: Allow DNS resolution (CoreDNS + GKE NodeLocal DNSCache)
   146→# =============================================================================
   147→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowDNS }}
   148→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   149→kind: NetworkPolicy
   150→metadata:
   151→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-dns
   152→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
   153→  labels:
   154→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
   155→  annotations:
   156→    description: "Allow DNS resolution via CoreDNS and NodeLocal DNSCache"
   157→spec:
   158→  podSelector:
   159→    matchLabels:
   160→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   161→  policyTypes:
   162→    - Egress
   163→  egress:
   164→    # CoreDNS in kube-system
   165→    - to:
   166→        - namespaceSelector:
   167→            matchLabels:
   168→              kubernetes.io/metadata.name: kube-system
   169→      ports:
   170→        - protocol: UDP
   171→          port: 53
   172→        - protocol: TCP
   173→          port: 53
   174→    # GKE NodeLocal DNSCache
   175→    - to:
   176→        - ipBlock:
   177→            cidr: 169.254.20.10/32
   178→      ports:
   179→        - protocol: UDP
   180→          port: 53
   181→        - protocol: TCP
   182→          port: 53
   183→{{- end }}
   184→
   185→---
   186→# =============================================================================
   187→# Network Policy 7: Allow GKE Metadata Server Egress
   188→# Purpose: Allow Workload Identity token exchange via GKE metadata server
   189→# =============================================================================
   190→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowMetadata }}
   191→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   192→kind: NetworkPolicy
   193→metadata:
   194→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-metadata
   195→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
   196→  labels:
   197→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
   198→  annotations:
   199→    description: "Allow egress to GKE metadata server for Workload Identity"
   200→spec:
   201→  podSelector:
   202→    matchLabels:
   203→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   204→  policyTypes:
   205→    - Egress
   206→  egress:
   207→    - to:
   208→        - ipBlock:
   209→            cidr: 169.254.169.254/32
   210→      ports:
   211→        - protocol: TCP
   212→          port: 80
   213→        - protocol: TCP
   214→          port: 988
   215→{{- end }}
   216→
   217→---
   218→# =============================================================================
   219→# Network Policy 8: Allow Kubernetes API Server Egress
   220→# Purpose: Allow crew provisioning and configmap access via K8s API
   221→# =============================================================================
   222→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowK8sAPI }}
   223→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   224→kind: NetworkPolicy
   225→metadata:
   226→  name: {{ include "crewai-gateway.fullname" . }}-allow-k8s-api
   227→  namespace: {{ include "crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace" . }}
   228→  labels:
   229→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
   230→  annotations:
   231→    description: "Allow egress to Kubernetes API server"
   232→spec:
   233→  podSelector:
   234→    matchLabels:
   235→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   236→  policyTypes:
   237→    - Egress
   238→  egress:
   239→    - to:
   240→        - ipBlock:
   241→            cidr: {{ .Values.networkPolicies.k8sAPICIDR }}
   242→      ports:
   243→        - protocol: TCP
   244→          port: 443
   245→{{- end }}
   246→
   247→---
   248→# =============================================================================
   249→# Network Policy 9: Allow External HTTPS Egress
   250→# Purpose: Allow egress to internet (GCS, WorkOS, external APIs) excluding RFC1918
   251→# =============================================================================
   252→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowInternetEgress }}
   253→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   254→kind: NetworkPolicy
   255→metadata:

   260→  annotations:
   261→    description: "Allow HTTPS egress to internet excluding RFC1918 ranges"
   262→spec:
   263→  podSelector:
   264→    matchLabels:
   265→      app.kubernetes.io/name: crewai
   266→  policyTypes:
   267→    - Egress
   268→  egress:
   269→    - to:
   270→        - ipBlock:
   271→            cidr: 0.0.0.0/0
   272→            except:
   273→              - 10.0.0.0/8
   274→              - 172.16.0.0/12
   275→              - 192.168.0.0/16
   276→      ports:
   277→        - protocol: TCP
   278→          port: 443
   279→{{- end }}
   280→
   281→---
   282→# =============================================================================
   283→# Network Policy 10: Allow Cloud SQL Proxy Egress
   284→# Purpose: Allow egress to Cloud SQL Proxy (in-namespace) for database access
   285→# =============================================================================
   286→{{- if .Values.networkPolicies.allowCloudSQLProxy }}
   287→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1
   288→kind: NetworkPolicy
   289→metadata:

   292→  labels:
   293→    {{- include "crewai-gateway.labels" . | nindent 4 }}
   294→  annotations:
   295→    description: "Allow egress to Cloud SQL Proxy pods on port 5432"
   296→spec:
   297→  podSelector:

   302→  egress:
   303→    - to:
   304→        - podSelector:
   305→            matchLabels:
   306→              app: cloud-sql-proxy
   307→      ports:
   308→        - protocol: TCP
   309→          port: 5432
   310→{{- end }}
   311→
   312→---
   313→# =============================================================================

   340→        - protocol: {{ .protocol }}

   342→        {{- end }}
   343→{{- end }}
   344→
   345→---

   372→
   373→# #############################################################################
   374→#
   375→#                    REPLICATED SDK NETWORK POLICIES
   376→#
   377→# #############################################################################
   378→# Replicated SDK needs: DNS, GKE metadata, K8s API, external HTTPS.
   379→# Separated from crewai app pods because replicated has different labels.
   380→# Only enabled when NOT in airgap mode (airgap has no external connectivity).
   381→
   382→{{- if and .Values.networkPolicies.replicated.enabled (not .Values.replicated.isAirgap) }}
   383→---
   384→# =============================================================================
   385→# Network Policy 13: Replicated - Default Deny Ingress
   386→# Purpose: Deny all inbound traffic by default for replicated SDK pods
   387→# Note: Egress is NOT denied here (matches crewai app pod pattern).
   388→# ipBlock rules against K8s API ClusterIP don't work reliably with
   389→# explicit egress deny on GKE Dataplane V2 (Cilium DNAT ordering).
   390→# =============================================================================
   391→apiVersion: networking.k8s.io/v1

   393→metadata:

   400→spec:

   403→      app.kubernetes.io/name: replicated
   404→  policyTypes:
   405→    - Ingress
   406→
   407→---

   434→          port: 53
   435→        - protocol: TCP
   436→          port: 53

> AGENT

Your analysis is correct that the 401 means "no valid identity presented" rather than "insufficient permissions." After reviewing the full codebase, I see **two separate problems** - the 401 authentication issue, and a missing RBAC gap that would surface as 403 once auth is resolved.

## The 401: Authentication Failure

The SA token is either invalid or not being presented. From the code, the most concerning pattern is **dual SA management with ServerSideApply**:

1. The local chart creates `crewai-sa` as a **PreSync hook** at wave -35 ([serviceaccount.yaml:17-18](environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/serviceaccount.yaml#L17-L18))
2. The upstream chart (`crewai-platform` from `registry.crewai.com`) almost certainly also manages a ServiceAccount resource for `crewai-sa` as a regular (non-hook) resource
3. The ArgoCD app uses `ServerSideApply=true` ([application.yaml:62](environments/dev/argocd/application.yaml#L62))

With SSA, both sources claim field ownership on the same SA. If a sync causes the SA to be deleted and recreated (UID change), all running pods hold tokens bound to the old UID - the API server rejects them as 401.

Additionally, the local chart's SA template has **no `hook-delete-policy`** annotation. Compare with [rbac-post-install-setup.yaml:21](environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/rbac-post-install-setup.yaml#L21) which explicitly sets `BeforeHookCreation`. Without this, the hook lifecycle is ambiguous during syncs.

**Recommended debugging (in order):**

```bash
# 1. Check if the SA exists and get its UID
kubectl get sa crewai-sa -n crewai -o jsonpath='{.metadata.uid}'

# 2. Exec into a worker pod and check the token's bound SA UID
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  cat /var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount/token | \
  cut -d. -f2 | base64 -d 2>/dev/null | python3 -m json.tool
# Compare the "kubernetes.io/serviceaccount/uid" claim with step 1

# 3. Check if the token is actually being used by the Ansible client
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  ls -la /var/run/secrets/kubernetes.io/serviceaccount/

# 4. Check the audit log for that specific Audit-ID
# Look for what identity (or lack thereof) was presented

# 5. Check if both charts manage the same SA (field manager conflict)
kubectl get sa crewai-sa -n crewai -o json | python3 -c \
  "import sys,json; [print(m['manager']) for m in json.load(sys.stdin).get('metadata',{}).get('managedFields',[])]"
```

If step 2 shows a UID mismatch with step 1, that confirms the SA was recreated. Fix: restart the worker pods (`kubectl rollout restart deploy/crewai-worker -n crewai`).

## The RBAC Gap (Blocking Issue Once 401 Is Resolved)

[CREW_NAMESPACE.md:50-60](docs/CREW_NAMESPACE.md#L50-L60) documents that `crewai-sa` should have crew management permissions in `crewai-crews`, but **no Role or RoleBinding exists anywhere in the codebase**. The referenced file `kubernetes/kustomize/base/rbac/crew-access-role.yaml` doesn't exist. Once the 401 is resolved, every K8s API call from the worker will hit **403 Forbidden** instead.

The SA `crewai-sa` currently has:
- Workload Identity binding to GCP SA (GCS, Cloud SQL, Artifact Registry)
- Network policy allowing K8s API egress
- `automountServiceAccountToken: true`
- **Zero RBAC permissions** in any namespace

Would you like me to create the RBAC template to fix the authorization gap? Based on the docs, the worker needs permissions to create/manage pods, deployments, configmaps, secrets, services, and jobs in `crewai-crews`, plus pod management in `crewai` for the BuildKit operations.

> AGENT

This is very informative. The token investigation eliminates SA recreation as the cause:

- **SA UID**: `cae10fd2-73ff-4dc9-96a9-d4de4958eeec`
- **Token SA UID**: `cae10fd2-73ff-4dc9-96a9-d4de4958eeec` -- **match**
- **Token expiry**: `1802513820` (far future, not expired)
- **Token refreshed**: `2026_02_13_10_17_00` (recently)
- **Mount**: all files present (ca.crt, namespace, token)

So the SA is intact, the token is valid and fresh, and the mount is correct. **The infrastructure layer is not the problem.** The 401 must be coming from the application not presenting the token to the API server.

## Revised Root Cause: Application K8s Client Config

Since the token is valid but the API server returns 401, the CrewAI worker's Ansible/Python K8s client is either not loading in-cluster credentials or is using a different auth path. The Ansible `kubernetes.core.k8s` module tries this order:

1. Module params (`kubeconfig`, `host`, `api_key`)
2. Env vars (`KUBECONFIG`, `K8S_AUTH_KUBECONFIG`)
3. `~/.kube/config`
4. In-cluster config (last resort)

If any earlier step finds a file (even with stale creds), it won't fall through to in-cluster config.

## Next debugging steps (run from inside the worker pod):

```bash
# Check if any K8s auth env vars are set that might override in-cluster
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- env | grep -iE 'kube|k8s_auth'

# Check if a kubeconfig file exists in the container
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- ls -la /root/.kube/config 2>&1
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- ls -la /home/crewai/.kube/config 2>&1

# Verify in-cluster config works directly with the Python K8s client
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  python3 -c "
from kubernetes import client, config
config.load_incluster_config()
v1 = client.AuthenticationV1Api()
print('In-cluster config loaded successfully')
print('Host:', client.Configuration.get_default_copy().host)
"

# Also check field managers from step 5 (may not have printed)
kubectl get sa crewai-sa -n crewai -o json | \
  python3 -c "import sys,json; [print(m['manager']) for m in json.load(sys.stdin).get('metadata',{}).get('managedFields',[])]"
```

The `python3 -c` test is the critical one -- if that succeeds, in-cluster config works and the issue is how the Ansible playbook `buildkit_build.yml` loads credentials. If it fails with 401, there's something wrong with the token audience or API server configuration.

## RBAC: Still Needed Regardless

Even once the 401 is resolved, the worker will immediately hit **403 Forbidden**. The `crewai-sa` service account has zero RBAC bindings. Per [CREW_NAMESPACE.md:52-60](docs/CREW_NAMESPACE.md#L52-L60), it needs pod/deployment/configmap/secret management in `crewai-crews` -- but none of that exists. Want me to go ahead and create the RBAC template while you investigate the auth issue in the cluster?

Good -- only dev has the local chart templates; qa and prod just have `values.yaml`. I'll create the RBAC in dev now.

Before I write the template, a quick note on your latest debugging results: **in-cluster config loads successfully** (`Host: https://10.20.0.1:443`), no `K8S_AUTH_*` overrides, no kubeconfig files. So the Python K8s client *can* authenticate. That test only loaded config though -- it didn't make an actual API call. I'd recommend testing with an actual call to see if you get 401 or 403:

```bash
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  python3 -c "
from kubernetes import client, config
config.load_incluster_config()
v1 = client.CoreV1Api()
try:
    v1.list_namespaced_pod('crewai-crews')
except Exception as e:
    print(type(e).__name__, e.status, e.reason)
"
```

If that returns **403**, the 401 is coming from the Ansible layer not using in-cluster config. If it returns **401**, there's something deeper going on with the token audience.

Now, the RBAC template:

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/code/konecta/konecta-tic/kix-services-crewai/environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/rbac-crew-provisioner.yaml",
  "content": "{{- /*\nRBAC for crew provisioning in the crews namespace.\nGrants the platform service account (crewai-sa) permissions to manage\ncrew workload resources in the crewai-crews namespace.\n\nThis complements the upstream chart's provisioner-role (PreSync) which\ncovers the platform namespace. This Role covers the crews namespace\nwhere deployed crew workloads execute.\n*/ -}}\n{{- if .Values.crewRBAC.enabled }}\n---\napiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1\nkind: Role\nmetadata:\n  name: crew-provisioner-role\n  namespace: {{ .Values.crewNamespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: rbac\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-5\"\nrules:\n  # Pod lifecycle management for crew workloads\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"pods\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\", \"patch\"]\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"pods/exec\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\"]\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"pods/log\"]\n    verbs: [\"get\"]\n  # Deployment and workload management\n  - apiGroups: [\"apps\"]\n    resources: [\"deployments\", \"statefulsets\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\", \"patch\", \"update\"]\n  - apiGroups: [\"apps\"]\n    resources: [\"replicasets\"]\n    verbs: [\"get\", \"list\", \"watch\"]\n  # Job management\n  - apiGroups: [\"batch\"]\n    resources: [\"jobs\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\"]\n  # Configuration management\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"configmaps\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\", \"patch\"]\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"secrets\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\"]\n  # Service management\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"services\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\", \"patch\"]\n  # Storage management\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"persistentvolumeclaims\"]\n    verbs: [\"create\", \"delete\", \"get\", \"list\", \"watch\"]\n  # Events for debugging and monitoring\n  - apiGroups: [\"\"]\n    resources: [\"events\"]\n    verbs: [\"get\", \"list\", \"watch\"]\n---\napiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1\nkind: RoleBinding\nmetadata:\n  name: crew-provisioner-role-binding\n  namespace: {{ .Values.crewNamespace }}\n  labels:\n    {{- include \"crewai-gateway.labels\" . | nindent 4 }}\n    app.kubernetes.io/component: rbac\n  annotations:\n    argocd.argoproj.io/sync-wave: \"-5\"\nsubjects:\n  - kind: ServiceAccount\n    name: {{ .Values.serviceAccount }}\n    namespace: {{ include \"crewai-gateway.crewaiNamespace\" . }}\nroleRef:\n  kind: Role\n  name: crew-provisioner-role\n  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io\n{{- end }}\n"
}

> AGENT

Template renders cleanly. Here's what was created:

**New file:** [rbac-crew-provisioner.yaml](environments/dev/helm/crewai/templates/rbac-crew-provisioner.yaml) -- Role + RoleBinding in `crewai-crews` namespace granting `crewai-sa` (from `crewai` namespace) permissions to manage:

| Resource | Verbs |
|----------|-------|
| pods | create, delete, get, list, watch, patch |
| pods/exec | create |
| pods/log | get |
| deployments, statefulsets | create, delete, get, list, watch, patch, update |
| replicasets | get, list, watch |
| jobs | create, delete, get, list, watch |
| configmaps | create, delete, get, list, watch, patch |
| secrets | create, delete, get, list, watch |
| services | create, delete, get, list, watch, patch |
| persistentvolumeclaims | create, delete, get, list, watch |
| events | get, list, watch |

**Modified:** [values.yaml](environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml) -- added `crewRBAC.enabled: true` toggle (section B5) and updated the sync wave reference.

This complements the upstream chart's `provisioner-role` (which covers the `crewai` namespace). The sync wave `-5` ensures the RBAC exists before the main workloads deploy at wave `0`.

One thing to flag: these resources target `crewai-crews` namespace, which is outside the ArgoCD app's destination namespace (`crewai`). The ArgoCD `platform` project needs to allow the `crewai-crews` namespace, otherwise the sync will be rejected. If that's not already configured, you'll see a "namespace not permitted" error during sync.

{
  "command": "gh pr create --base main --head feat/crew-namespace-rbac --title \"feat: add crew namespace RBAC for worker provisioning\" --body \"$(cat <<'EOF'\n## Summary\n- Adds `crew-provisioner-role` Role and `crew-provisioner-role-binding` RoleBinding in the `crewai-crews` namespace\n- Grants `crewai-sa` (platform SA) permissions to manage pods, deployments, jobs, configmaps, secrets, services, and PVCs for crew workload provisioning\n- Complements the upstream chart's `provisioner-role` which only covers the `crewai` platform namespace\n- Adds `crewRBAC.enabled` toggle in values.yaml (section B5)\n\n## Context\nThe worker pod (`crewai-platform-worker`) was failing with **401 Unauthorized** when attempting to interact with the K8s API for crew provisioning via the Ansible `buildkit_build.yml` playbook. Investigation confirmed:\n\n1. The SA token is valid and UID-matched (not a token invalidation issue)\n2. In-cluster config loads successfully from within the worker pod\n3. **No RBAC existed** for `crewai-sa` in the `crewai-crews` namespace — the documented RBAC from `CREW_NAMESPACE.md` was never implemented\n\nThe 401 auth issue is being investigated separately (likely application-layer K8s client config). This PR resolves the RBAC gap that would otherwise surface as 403 once auth is fixed.\n\n## Test plan\n- [ ] Verify ArgoCD `platform` project allows `crewai-crews` namespace (required for cross-namespace resource management)\n- [ ] Confirm `crew-provisioner-role` and `crew-provisioner-role-binding` are created in `crewai-crews` after sync\n- [ ] Validate worker can create pods in `crewai-crews`: `kubectl auth can-i create pods -n crewai-crews --as=system:serviceaccount:crewai:crewai-sa`\n- [ ] Test crew provisioning end-to-end once the 401 auth issue is resolved\n- [ ] Replicate RBAC template to qa/prod environments after dev validation\n\n🤖 Generated with [Claude Code](https://claude.com/claude-code)\nEOF\n)\"",

> AGENT

Good diagnosis. That's a clear-cut permissions problem on the dynamically-created BuildKit build pod, separate from the 401 auth issue.

The static BuildKit deployment in our Helm values already has `fsGroup: 1000` set correctly ([values.yaml:258](environments/dev/helm/crewai/values.yaml#L258)), so this is specifically about the **build pod** that the worker creates at runtime via the Ansible playbook. That pod spec is controlled by the upstream CrewAI application code, not our Helm chart.

Two questions:

1. **Does the upstream chart expose a config option for the build pod's securityContext?** Something like `buildkit.buildPodSecurityContext` or similar in the chart values. If so, we can set `fsGroup: 1000` from our values.yaml. If not, this needs an upstream fix or a patch to the Ansible playbook in the container image.
