Based on my thorough exploration, let me now compile a comprehensive report of my findings:

## Comprehensive CrewAI Helm Chart Analysis

I've thoroughly explored the upstream CrewAI Helm chart at `/home/nathan_booth/code/crewai/crewai-platform`. Here are my detailed findings:

### 1. **BUILD POD SPEC - Dynamic Pod Creation**

The chart does **NOT contain templates for dynamically created build pods** with `buildkit-client` containers or `helper` init containers. The build system architecture is:

- **BuildKit Deployment** (`/templates/buildkit/deployment.yaml`): A single long-running `buildkitd` daemon pod that acts as the build server
- **PROVIDER Configuration** (`values.yaml` line 358): Set to `BUILDKIT_KUBERNETES`, meaning the application uses the BuildKit daemon to build container images
- **Worker Interaction**: The worker pods communicate with BuildKit via the TCP endpoint: `tcp://crewai-buildkit.{namespace}:{port}` (configured in `_helpers.tpl` line 437-438)

**Key Finding**: Build pods are NOT statically defined in the chart. They are created **dynamically by the application code** (the Rails app running in web/worker containers) when a build is triggered, using the Kubernetes API. The BuildKit daemon acts as the orchestrator for these dynamic builds.

### 2. **SECURITY CONTEXT CONFIGURATION FOR BUILDKIT**

**BuildKit Deployment SecurityContext** (`templates/buildkit/deployment.yaml` lines 26-107):

```yaml
# Pod-level securityContext (lines 26-36):
securityContext:
  runAsNonRoot: true
  runAsUser: 1000          # Configurable via .Values.buildkit.rootless.runAsUser
  runAsGroup: 1000         # Configurable via .Values.buildkit.rootless.runAsGroup
  fsGroup: 1000            # Configurable via .Values.buildkit.rootless.fsGroup
  seccompProfile:
    type: Unconfined
  appArmorProfile:
    type: Unconfined

# Container-level securityContext (lines 79-107):
securityContext:
  # Rootless mode (when .Values.buildkit.rootless.enabled = true)
  runAsNonRoot: true
  runAsUser: 1000          # Defaults to 1000
  runAsGroup: 1000         # Defaults to 1000
  allowPrivilegeEscalation: true  # Required for newuidmap/newgidmap setuid binaries
  seccompProfile:
    type: Unconfined
  appArmorProfile:
    type: Unconfined
  
  # OR User namespaces mode (when .Values.buildkit.userns.enabled = true)
  capabilities:
    add:
      - SYS_ADMIN
  seccompProfile:
    type: Unconfined
  
  # OR Standard privileged mode (default)
  privileged: true
```

**ConfigurationOptions** (`values.yaml` lines 88-125):
- `buildkit.rootless.enabled`: Boolean to enable rootless mode
- `buildkit.rootless.runAsUser`: User ID (default: 1000)
- `buildkit.rootless.runAsGroup`: Group ID (default: 1000)
- `buildkit.rootless.fsGroup`: File system group (default: 1000)
- `buildkit.userns.enabled`: User namespace mode
- `buildkit.runAsUser`: Non-rootless user ID (optional)
- `buildkit.runAsGroup`: Non-rootless group ID (optional)

### 3. **RBAC - PreSync Hooks**

**RBAC Templates** (`templates/rbac.yaml` and `templates/role.yaml`, `templates/rolebinding.yaml`):

**Role Definition** (`rbac.yaml` lines 5-44):
```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: Role
metadata:
  name: provisioner-role
  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
  annotations:
    "helm.sh/hook": pre-install,pre-upgrade
    "helm.sh/hook-weight": "-8"
rules:
  # Deployments, jobs, replicasets
  - apiGroups: ["apps"]
    resources: ["deployments", "jobs", "replicasets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # Services and Secrets
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["services", "secrets"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # Ingresses
  - apiGroups: ["networking.k8s.io"]
    resources: ["ingresses"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # Pods and PVCs (for creating helper pods, waiting on them, copying files)
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods", "persistentvolumeclaims"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # pods/exec subresource (for file copying)
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["pods/exec"]
    verbs: ["create", "get", "list", "watch", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # Jobs (for Kaniko builds)
  - apiGroups: ["batch"]
    resources: ["jobs"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
  
  # ConfigMaps (for storing release info)
  - apiGroups: [""]
    resources: ["configmaps"]
    verbs: ["get", "list", "watch", "create", "update", "patch", "delete"]
```

**RoleBinding Definition** (`rbac.yaml` lines 47-62):
```yaml
apiVersion: rbac.authorization.k8s.io/v1
kind: RoleBinding
metadata:
  name: provisioner-role-binding
  namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
  annotations:
    "helm.sh/hook": pre-install,pre-upgrade
    "helm.sh/hook-weight": "-8"
subjects:
  - kind: ServiceAccount
    name: default
    namespace: {{ .Release.Namespace }}
roleRef:
  kind: Role
  name: provisioner-role
  apiGroup: rbac.authorization.k8s.io
```

**Application-level Roles** (`role.yaml` lines 1-40):
- Main application role: Grants **all permissions** (`*/*`) in the release namespace
- Crew namespace role: Grants **all permissions** in the crew namespace (when different from release namespace)

### 4. **WORKER INTERACTION WITH K8S API**

**Worker Deployment** (`templates/worker/deployment.yaml`):
- No init containers
- No special K8s API configuration
- Uses service account specified by `include "crewai-platform.serviceAccountName"` (lines 22-24)

**Key Environment Variables** (`_helpers.tpl` lines 424-471):
```yaml
- name: K8S_NAMESPACE
  value: "{{ .Values.crewNamespace }}"          # Crew execution namespace
- name: BUILDKIT_HOST
  value: "tcp://crewai-buildkit.{namespace}:{port}"  # BuildKit service endpoint
- name: BUILDKIT_IMAGE_OVERRIDE
  value: "{{ buildkit_image_url }}"              # Image for dynamic build pods
```

**What the worker does**:
- Connects to BuildKit daemon via TCP
- Requests builds for crew containers
- BuildKit creates ephemeral build pods dynamically in the cluster
- No direct Ansible playbooks or buildkit_build.yml files in the chart

### 5. **CHART VALUES CONFIGURATION**

**Key Sections** (`values.yaml`):

**Buildkit Configuration** (lines 88-145):
```yaml
buildkit:
  enabled: true
  replicaCount: 1
  image:
    name: "proxy/crewai/crewai/crewai/buildkit"
    tag: "v2026.0130.11"
  rootless:
    enabled: false
    runAsUser: 1000
    runAsGroup: 1000
    fsGroup: 1000
  oci:
    enabled: true
  containerd:
    enabled: false
  debug: false

      cpu: "4"

  healthcheck:
    enabled: true
```

**BuildKit ConfigMap** (`templates/buildkit/configmap.yaml`):
- `buildkitd.toml`: BuildKit daemon configuration
- Registry settings for pulling base images
- OCI worker configuration (rootless settings)
- Subuid/subgid for user namespace remapping (lines 10-17)

**Environmental Variables** (`values.yaml` lines 309-399):
```yaml
envVars:
  PROVIDER: BUILDKIT_KUBERNETES              # Build orchestrator
  BUILDKIT_HOST: (auto-configured)            # BuildKit daemon endpoint
  BUILDKIT_IMAGE_OVERRIDE: (auto-configured)  # Image for dynamic build pods
  CONTAINER_REGISTRY_HOSTNAME: (auto-configured)
  CREW_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: ""            # REQUIRED - where to push crew images
  AUTOMATION_BASE_IMAGE_REGISTRY_OVERRIDE: "" # Registry for base images
  AUTOMATION_ECR_REPOSITORY_PREFIX: ""        # Path prefix in registry
  AUTOMATION_BASE_IMAGE_NAME: "crewai-enterprise-preinstalled-v2:latest"
```

### 6. **WORKSPACE AND VOLUME CONFIGURATION**

The chart does NOT define workspace volumes for dynamic build pods. Based on the RBAC rules mentioning "helper pods" and references in the preflight checks (line 251: "Build Artifacts: 2-10Gi (if using Kaniko image building)"), builds use:

**In BuildKit Deployment** (`templates/buildkit/deployment.yaml` lines 151-160):
```yaml
volumes:
  - name: buildkit-cache
    emptyDir: {}              # Cache directory for BuildKit
  - name: config
    configMap:
      name: crewai-buildkit-config
      readOnly: true
  - name: docker-config
    secret:
      secretName: docker-registry
      readOnly: true
```

**Mount Points**:
- **Rootless mode**: `/home/user/.local/share/buildkit`
- **Standard mode**: `/var/lib/buildkit`

### 7. **KEY MISSING COMPONENTS**

The chart intentionally does NOT include:
- **buildkit-client** containers (the daemon does this)
- **helper** init containers (not in static templates)
- **buildkit_build.yml** Ansible playbooks (application code responsibility)
- **Dynamic workspace pod templates** (created by application at runtime)

The dynamic build pod creation is entirely **handled by the application code** (Rails web/worker processes) through the Kubernetes API, not through chart-defined templates.

> AGENT

This changes things significantly. The upstream chart already handles RBAC for both namespaces. Here's what it creates when `rbac.create: true` (the default):

**PreSync hooks** ([rbac.yaml](file:///home/nathan_booth/code/crewai/crewai-platform/templates/rbac.yaml)):
- `provisioner-role` + `provisioner-role-binding` in `crewai` namespace, bound to **`default` SA** (for helm hook operations)

**Regular resources** ([role.yaml](file:///home/nathan_booth/code/crewai/crewai-platform/templates/role.yaml) + [rolebinding.yaml](file:///home/nathan_booth/code/crewai/crewai-platform/templates/rolebinding.yaml)):
- `crewai-role` → wildcard `*/*` in `crewai` namespace, bound to `crewai-sa`
- `crewai-crew-role` → wildcard `*/*` in `crewai-crews` namespace, bound to `crewai-sa`

Since `global.fullnameOverride: "crewai"` is set in our values, `{fullname}-sa` resolves to `crewai-sa` -- our exact SA name.

**So the upstream chart already grants `crewai-sa` full wildcard permissions in `crewai-crews`.** The RBAC template I created is redundant.

The real question is: **are these resources actually present in the cluster?** Can you check:

```bash
# Check if the upstream RBAC resources exist
kubectl get role -n crewai-crews
kubectl get rolebinding -n crewai-crews

# Check if rbac.create is being passed correctly
kubectl get role crewai-role -n crewai -o yaml 2>&1 | head -5
kubectl get role crewai-crew-role -n crewai-crews -o yaml 2>&1 | head -5
```

If `crewai-crew-role` and `crewai-crew-rolebinding` exist in `crewai-crews`, then our PR #161 template is unnecessary and can be dropped. The 401 is purely an authentication issue, not RBAC.

If they DON'T exist, then either `rbac.create` is being overridden to `false` somewhere, or ArgoCD pruned them. Our values.yaml doesn't explicitly set `rbac.create`, so it should default to `true` from the upstream chart.

> AGENT

No references to Ansible, playbooks, or K8s client logic in the chart at all. The build pod creation is entirely inside the application container image. The chart just wires up `K8S_NAMESPACE`, `BUILDKIT_HOST`, and the service account.

At this point, the infrastructure is verified correct. The 401 investigation needs to go inside the application code. Here's what to check:

```bash
# Find the Ansible playbook inside the container
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  find / -name "buildkit_build.yml" -o -name "buildkit_build.yaml" 2>/dev/null

# Check if there's a Ruby K8s client config or kubeconfig being generated
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  find / -name "*.yml" -path "*/ansible/*" 2>/dev/null | head -20

# Actually test a K8s API call (not just config loading) to see if you get 401 or 403
kubectl exec -n crewai deploy/crewai-worker -- \
  python3 -c "
from kubernetes import client, config
config.load_incluster_config()
v1 = client.CoreV1Api()
try:
    pods = v1.list_namespaced_pod('crewai-crews')
    print('SUCCESS:', len(pods.items), 'pods')
except Exception as e:
    print(type(e).__name__, e.status, e.reason)
"
```

The last command is the critical one. If it returns **SUCCESS**, then the Python K8s client works fine with in-cluster auth and the 401 must be coming from how Ansible invokes the client (maybe with explicit params that override in-cluster config). If it returns **401**, there's something deeper going on with the token audience or API server validation.

> AGENT

No chart-level configuration exists for the dynamic build pod's securityContext. The build pod spec is hardcoded in the application code inside the container image, not in the Helm chart.

The chart only controls:
- The **static BuildKit daemon** deployment (which already has `fsGroup: 1000` via `buildkit.rootless`)
- Environment variables passed to the worker (`BUILDKIT_IMAGE_OVERRIDE`, `BUSYBOX_IMAGE_OVERRIDE`, `K8S_NAMESPACE`)

The dynamic build pod spec (with the `helper` init container, `buildkit-client` main container, and `workspace` emptyDir) is constructed by the application at runtime.

Is the CrewAI application source available somewhere? Let me check.

No application source code here -- these are all infra/deployment repos. The build pod spec is baked into the CrewAI container image.

Three options to fix the `fsGroup` issue:

**1. Kyverno mutating policy (if available)** -- intercept build pods and inject `fsGroup: 1000` at admission time. No upstream change needed.

**2. Upstream fix request** -- ask CrewAI to add `fsGroup: 1000` to the dynamic build pod spec in their application code (or expose it as a chart value).

**3. Patch the container image** -- find `buildkit_build.yml` inside the worker image and modify the pod spec.

Do you have Kyverno or any mutating admission controller installed on the cluster? That would be the fastest path -- a policy that matches build pods in the `crewai` namespace and sets `spec.securityContext.fsGroup: 1000`.

> DEVELOPER

tell me more about option 1

> AGENT

Kyverno is a Kubernetes-native policy engine that runs as an admission controller. When a pod creation request hits the API server, Kyverno intercepts it and can **mutate** the spec before it's persisted -- the application never knows its pod was modified.

## How it would work

The CrewAI worker calls the K8s API to create a build pod. Before the pod is actually created, Kyverno's webhook intercepts the request and injects `fsGroup: 1000` into `spec.securityContext`. The pod gets created with the correct permissions, and the `helper` init container's root-owned files become group-readable by GID 1000.

## The policy

A namespace-scoped `Policy` targeting build pods in `crewai`:

```yaml
apiVersion: kyverno.io/v1
kind: Policy
metadata:
  name: buildkit-build-pod-fsgroup
  namespace: crewai
spec:
  rules:
    - name: add-fsgroup-to-build-pods
      match:
        any:
          - resources:
              kinds:
                - Pod
              # Match pods with the buildkit-client container
              operations:
                - CREATE
      preconditions:
        all:
          # Only mutate pods that have a container named buildkit-client
          - key: "buildkit-client"
            operator: AnyIn
            value: "{{ request.object.spec.containers[].name }}"
      mutate:
        patchStrategicMerge:
          spec:
            securityContext:
              fsGroup: 1000
              fsGroupChangePolicy: OnRootMismatch
```

**What this does:**
- Matches only pod CREATE requests in the `crewai` namespace
- Precondition narrows to pods with a container named `buildkit-client` (avoids mutating unrelated pods)
- Patches `spec.securityContext.fsGroup: 1000` using strategic merge (additive, won't clobber existing fields like `seccompProfile`)
- `fsGroupChangePolicy: OnRootMismatch` is a performance optimization -- only chown files that don't already match

## Prerequisites

Check if Kyverno is installed:

```bash
kubectl get pods -n kyverno 2>/dev/null || kubectl get pods --all-namespaces | grep kyverno
```

If it's not installed, it can be added via Helm:

```bash
helm repo add kyverno https://kyverno.github.io/kyverno/
helm install kyverno kyverno/kyverno -n kyverno --create-namespace
```

Though on a managed platform you'd want to go through your standard deployment process for cluster-level components.

## Testing without Kyverno

You can also dry-run the concept by creating a test pod manually with the fixed securityContext to confirm `fsGroup: 1000` resolves the `lstat` permission denied:

```bash
# Recreate the build pod scenario with fsGroup set
kubectl run test-fsgroup -n crewai --rm -it \
  --image=busybox --restart=Never \
  --overrides='{
    "spec": {
      "securityContext": {"fsGroup": 1000},
      "containers": [{"name": "test", "image": "busybox", 
        "command": ["sh", "-c", "touch /workspace/testfile && ls -la /workspace/"],
        "volumeMounts": [{"name": "ws", "mountPath": "/workspace"}]}],
      "volumes": [{"name": "ws", "emptyDir": {}}]
    }
  }'
```

> AGENT

Here's a diagnostic report you can send to CrewAI:

---

**Bug Report: BuildKit Build Pod - Permission Denied on Workspace Volume**

**Environment**
- CrewAI Enterprise Helm chart: `crewai-platform` v0.3.13
- Platform: GKE Autopilot (Dataplane V2 / Cilium)
- Kubernetes: GKE, `baseline` Pod Security Standards enforced
- BuildKit provider: `BUILDKIT_KUBERNETES`
- Rootless mode: enabled (`buildkit.rootless.enabled: true`)
- Namespace: `crewai` (platform), `crewai-crews` (crew workloads)

**Symptom**

When the worker triggers a crew build, the dynamically-created BuildKit build pod fails with:

```
lstat /workspace/<path>: permission denied
```

The `buildkit-client` container cannot read files written by the `helper` init container on the shared `workspace` emptyDir volume.

**Root Cause**

The dynamically-created build pod has a UID mismatch between the init container and main container, with no `fsGroup` set to bridge the gap:

| Container | Type | UID | GID |
|-----------|------|-----|-----|
| `helper` | init | 0 (root) | 0 (root) |
| `buildkit-client` | main | 1000 | 1000 |

The pod-level `securityContext` only sets:

```yaml
securityContext:
  seccompProfile:
    type: RuntimeDefault
```

No `fsGroup` is configured. As a result:
1. The `helper` init container writes files to `/workspace` owned by `root:root` (0:0)
2. The `buildkit-client` container starts as UID 1000 and cannot `lstat` the root-owned files
3. The `workspace` volume is additionally mounted `readOnly: true` in the main container, preventing any runtime permission fix

**Static BuildKit daemon is not affected** -- the `buildkit/deployment.yaml` template correctly sets `fsGroup: 1000` via `buildkit.rootless.fsGroup`. The issue is only in the dynamically-created build pods, whose spec is constructed by the application at runtime and does not inherit these settings.

**Proposed Fix**

Add `fsGroup: 1000` to the dynamically-created build pod's `spec.securityContext`:

```yaml
securityContext:
  seccompProfile:
    type: RuntimeDefault
  fsGroup: 1000
  fsGroupChangePolicy: OnRootMismatch
```

This causes Kubernetes to set group ownership of the emptyDir to GID 1000, making all files group-readable regardless of which UID wrote them.

Ideally, expose this as a chart value (e.g., `buildkit.buildPod.securityContext.fsGroup`) so operators can configure it to match their environment's security policy.

**Alternative**: have the `helper` init container run as UID/GID 1000 instead of root, or `chmod -R o+rX /workspace` before writing the `INIT_DONE` sentinel.

**Workaround**

We are currently evaluating a Kyverno mutating admission policy to inject `fsGroup: 1000` into build pods matching the `buildkit-client` container name at admission time.

> AGENT

Good catch on both counts, especially the `PIPESTATUS` / `set -o pipefail` issue -- that's a nasty silent failure. Want me to produce an updated diagnostic for CrewAI that covers both bugs? Here's the consolidated version:

---

**Bug Report: BuildKit Build Pod - Permission Denied + Exit Code Masking**

**Environment**
- CrewAI Enterprise Helm chart: `crewai-platform` v0.3.13
- Platform: GKE Autopilot (Dataplane V2 / Cilium)
- Kubernetes: GKE, `baseline` Pod Security Standards enforced
- BuildKit provider: `BUILDKIT_KUBERNETES`
- Rootless mode: enabled (`buildkit.rootless.enabled: true`, UID 1000)
- Build architecture: `buildkit-client` pod connects to remote daemon at `tcp://crewai-buildkit.crewai:1234`

---

### Bug 1: Permission Denied on Build Context

**Symptom**

```
lstat /workspace/Dockerfile: permission denied
```

The `buildctl` client in the `buildkit-client` container cannot read the build context files from the `/workspace` emptyDir volume.

**Root Cause**

The build pod's `/workspace` volume is populated in two stages with mismatched UIDs:

1. The `helper` init container starts and waits for `/workspace/INIT_DONE`
2. Ansible (running in the worker pod) uses `kubectl exec` to copy source code into the `helper` init container's `/workspace`. The copy runs as the init container's UID -- **root (0:0)**
3. The `buildkit-client` main container starts as **UID 1000:GID 1000** and runs `buildctl build --local context=/workspace --local dockerfile=/workspace`
4. `buildctl` attempts to read the root-owned files and gets `EACCES`

**Contributing Factors**
- No `fsGroup` on the pod-level `securityContext` -- Kubernetes does not adjust group ownership of the emptyDir
- Files written by the Ansible copy inherit the init container's UID (root), not the main container's UID (1000)
- The `/workspace` mount is `readOnly: true` in the main container, preventing any runtime `chmod`

**Fix Options (in order of preference)**

1. Add `fsGroup: 1000` to the dynamic build pod's `spec.securityContext`:
   ```yaml
   securityContext:
     fsGroup: 1000
     fsGroupChangePolicy: OnRootMismatch
   ```
   This makes all emptyDir files group-readable by GID 1000 regardless of owning UID.

2. Run the `helper` init container as UID 1000 instead of root, so files are owned by 1000:1000 from the start.

3. Add a `chmod -R o+rX /workspace` step after the Ansible source copy completes and before writing `INIT_DONE`.

4. Expose the build pod's `securityContext` as a chart value (e.g., `buildkit.buildPod.securityContext`) so operators can configure it per environment.

---

### Bug 2: Build Exit Code Masked by Pipeline

**Symptom**

Build logs show clear failures (permission denied, context load errors), but the build reports:

```
Build completed with exit code: 0
```

**Root Cause**

The build script pipes `buildctl` output through `sed` for log formatting:

```bash
buildctl build ... 2>&1 | sed 's/.../.../g'
```

In bash, `$?` captures the exit code of the **last command in the pipeline** (`sed`), not `buildctl`. Since `sed` successfully processed the error output, it exits 0, masking the `buildctl` failure.

**Fix**

Add `set -o pipefail` at the top of the build script:

```bash
#!/bin/bash
set -o pipefail

buildctl build ... 2>&1 | sed 's/.../.../g'
# $? now correctly reflects buildctl's exit code
```

Or use `PIPESTATUS` explicitly:

```bash
buildctl build ... 2>&1 | sed 's/.../.../g'
exit_code=${PIPESTATUS[0]}  # buildctl's exit code, not sed's
```

**Impact**

Without this fix, failed builds are silently reported as successful, causing downstream processes to proceed with missing or broken container images.

---

**Workaround**

We are evaluating a Kyverno mutating admission policy to inject `fsGroup: 1000` into dynamically-created build pods matching the `buildkit-client` container name. This addresses Bug 1 without requiring an application change. Bug 2 has no infrastructure-level workaround.

---

### **Secondary Issues**

#### **PROD Environment - Same Issues as QA**

---

### **DEV vs QA/PROD Architecture Difference**

**DEV Environment:** Uses hardcoded credentials (SECURITY RISK)
- Lines 102-145 in `secrets.tf` contain inline hardcoded values

**QA & PROD Environments:** Uses variable-based approach (SECURE)
- Both environments reference `var.oauth_client_id`, `var.oauth_api_key`, `var.oauth_domain` 
- These are marked as `sensitive = true` in `variables.tf`
- Secrets should be injected via `terraform.tfvars` or environment variables

     1→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     2→# Secrets Management
     3→#
     4→# GCP Secret Manager secrets consumed by External Secrets Operator (ESO).
     5→# ESO syncs secrets from GCP SM to K8s via ExternalSecret + ClusterSecretStore.
     6→# ClusterSecretStore (gcp-secret-manager) is platform-managed - not in this code.
     7→# Uses the single-secret-per-call pattern for the secret-manager module.
     8→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
     9→
    10→# NOTE: Database uses IAM authentication via Cloud SQL Proxy - no password needed
    11→
    12→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    13→# Secret Manager: Rails Secrets
    14→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    15→# SECRET_KEY_BASE for Rails session encryption and signed cookies.
    16→# Replicates Helm chart behavior: 64-character alphanumeric string.
    17→# Terraform state preserves the value across applies (equivalent to Helm's
    18→# existing secret lookup pattern).
    19→#
    20→# ENCRYPTION_KEY for ActiveRecord encryption (hex format, 64 chars = 32 bytes).
    21→#
    22→# Active Record Encryption keys (64 chars each, matching Helm chart):
    23→# - ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_PRIMARY_KEY
    24→# - ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_DETERMINISTIC_KEY
    25→# - ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_KEY_DERIVATION_SALT
    26→
    27→resource "random_password" "rails_secret_key_base" {
    28→  length  = 64
    29→  special = false
    30→}
    31→
    32→resource "random_password" "active_record_encryption_primary_key" {
    33→  length  = 64
    34→  special = false
    35→}
    36→
    37→resource "random_password" "active_record_encryption_deterministic_key" {
    38→  length  = 64
    39→  special = false
    40→}
    41→
    42→resource "random_password" "active_record_encryption_key_derivation_salt" {
    43→  length  = 64
    44→  special = false
    45→}
    46→
    47→resource "random_id" "encryption_key" {
    48→  byte_length = 32
    49→}
    50→
    51→module "secret_rails_secrets" {
    52→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    53→
    54→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
    55→  environment = var.environment
    56→  secret_id   = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
    57→  secret_data = jsonencode({
    58→    secret_key_base                              = random_password.rails_secret_key_base.result
    59→    encryption_key                               = random_id.encryption_key.hex
    60→    active_record_encryption_primary_key         = random_password.active_record_encryption_primary_key.result
    61→    active_record_encryption_deterministic_key   = random_password.active_record_encryption_deterministic_key.result
    62→    active_record_encryption_key_derivation_salt = random_password.active_record_encryption_key_derivation_salt.result
    63→  })
    64→
    65→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
    66→  additional_labels         = local.labels
    67→}
    68→
    69→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    70→# Secret Manager: GCS Credentials (HMAC Keys)
    71→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    72→# HMAC keys for S3-compatible GCS access (used by Rails ActiveStorage).
    73→
    74→module "secret_gcs_credentials" {
    75→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    76→
    77→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
    78→  environment = var.environment
    79→  secret_id   = "crewai-gcs-credentials-${var.environment}"
    80→  secret_data = jsonencode({
    81→    access_key_id     = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.access_id
    82→    secret_access_key = google_storage_hmac_key.crewai.secret
    83→  })
    84→
    85→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
    86→  additional_labels         = local.labels
    87→}
    88→
    89→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    90→# Secret Manager: WorkOS OAuth Credentials
    91→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
    92→# WorkOS authentication credentials for SSO integration.
    93→# Note: These are development/staging credentials.
    94→
    95→module "secret_workos_credentials" {
    96→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
    97→
    98→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
    99→  environment = var.environment
   100→  secret_id   = "crewai-workos-credentials-${var.environment}"
   101→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   102→    client_id       = "client_01KCK4VFR0M8190ASEATDR8BQF"
   103→    api_key=[REDACTED]"
   104→    cookie_password=[REDACTED]"
   105→    authkit_domain  = "undisputed-tip-18-staging.authkit.app"
   106→  })
   107→
   108→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
   109→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   110→}
   111→
   112→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   113→# Secret Manager: GitHub App Credentials
   114→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   115→# GitHub App credentials for repository integration.
   116→# Note: These are development credentials for the crewai-git-dev GitHub App.
   117→
   118→module "secret_github_credentials" {
   119→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   120→
   121→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
   122→  environment = var.environment
   123→  secret_id   = "crewai-github-credentials-${var.environment}"
   124→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   125→    client_secret=[REDACTED]"
   126→    app_private_key = "[REDACTED_PRIVATE_KEY]"
   127→  })
   128→
   129→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
   130→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   131→}
   132→
   133→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   134→# Secret Manager: Replicated SDK Credentials
   135→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   136→# Replicated SDK auth token for license validation and update checks.
   137→
   138→module "secret_replicated_credentials" {
   139→  source = "github.com/konecta-ix-platform/kix-platform-modules//terraform/gcp/secret-manager?ref=main"
   140→
   141→  project_id  = var.security_project_id
   142→  environment = var.environment
   143→  secret_id   = "crewai-replicated-credentials-${var.environment}"
   144→  secret_data = jsonencode({
   145→    auth_token=[REDACTED]"
   146→  })
   147→
   148→  accessor_service_accounts = [local.crewai_sa_email]
   149→  additional_labels         = local.labels
   150→}
   151→
   152→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   153→# ExternalSecret: CrewAI Application Credentials
   154→# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
   155→# Uses ClusterSecretStore (platform-managed) to sync secrets from GCP SM.
   156→# ESO creates the K8s secret automatically - no direct secret creation needed.
   157→# The ClusterSecretStore 'gcp-secret-manager' is managed at platform level.
   158→
   159→resource "kubernetes_manifest" "external_secret_crewai_credentials" {
   160→  manifest = {
   161→    apiVersion = "external-secrets.io/v1beta1"
   162→    kind       = "ExternalSecret"
   163→    metadata = {
   164→      name      = "crewai-credentials"
   165→      namespace = local.platform_namespace
   166→      labels = {
   167→        app         = "crewai"
   168→        environment = var.environment
   169→        managed-by  = "terraform"
   170→      }
   171→    }
   172→    spec = {
   173→      refreshInterval = "1h"
   174→      secretStoreRef = {
   175→        kind = "ClusterSecretStore"
   176→        name = "gcp-secret-manager"
   177→      }
   178→      target = {
   179→        name           = "crewai-secrets"
   180→        creationPolicy = "Owner"
   181→      }
   182→      data = [
   183→        {
   184→          secretKey = "SECRET_KEY_BASE"
   185→          remoteRef = {
   186→            key      = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
   187→            property = "secret_key_base"
   188→          }
   189→        },
   190→        {
   191→          secretKey = "ENCRYPTION_KEY"
   192→          remoteRef = {
   193→            key      = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
   194→            property = "encryption_key"
   195→          }
   196→        },
   197→        {
   198→          secretKey = "ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_PRIMARY_KEY"
   199→          remoteRef = {
   200→            key      = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
   201→            property = "active_record_encryption_primary_key"
   202→          }
   203→        },
   204→        {
   205→          secretKey = "ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_DETERMINISTIC_KEY"
   206→          remoteRef = {
   207→            key      = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
   208→            property = "active_record_encryption_deterministic_key"
   209→          }
   210→        },
   211→        {
   212→          secretKey = "ACTIVE_RECORD_ENCRYPTION_KEY_DERIVATION_SALT"
   213→          remoteRef = {
   214→            key      = "crewai-rails-secrets-${var.environment}"
   215→            property = "active_record_encryption_key_derivation_salt"
   216→          }
   217→        },
   218→        {
   219→          secretKey = "AWS_ACCESS_KEY_ID"
   220→          remoteRef = {
   221→            key      = "crewai-gcs-credentials-${var.environment}"
   222→            property = "access_key_id"
   223→          }
   224→        },
   225→        {
   226→          secretKey = "AWS_SECRET_ACCESS_KEY"
   227→          remoteRef = {
   228→            key      = "crewai-gcs-credentials-${var.environment}"
   229→            property = "secret_access_key"
   230→          }
   231→        },
   232→        {
   233→          secretKey = "WORKOS_CLIENT_ID"
   234→          remoteRef = {
   235→            key      = "crewai-workos-credentials-${var.environment}"
   236→            property = "client_id"
   237→          }
   238→        },
   239→        {
   240→          secretKey = "WORKOS_API_KEY"
   241→          remoteRef = {
   242→            key      = "crewai-workos-credentials-${var.environment}"
   243→            property = "api_key"
   244→          }
   245→        },
   246→        {
   247→          secretKey = "WORKOS_COOKIE_PASSWORD"
   248→          remoteRef = {
   249→            key      = "crewai-workos-credentials-${var.environment}"
   250→            property = "cookie_password"
   251→          }
   252→        },
   253→        {
   254→          secretKey = "WORKOS_AUTHKIT_DOMAIN"
   255→          remoteRef = {
   256→            key      = "crewai-workos-credentials-${var.environment}"
   257→            property = "authkit_domain"
   258→          }
   259→        },
   260→        {
   261→          secretKey = "GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET"
   262→          remoteRef = {
   263→            key      = "crewai-github-credentials-${var.environment}"
   264→            property = "client_secret"
   265→          }
   266→        },
   267→        {
   268→          secretKey = "GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY"
   269→          remoteRef = {
   270→            key      = "crewai-github-credentials-${var.environment}"
   271→            property = "app_private_key"
   272→          }
   273→        },
   274→        {
   275→          secretKey = "REPLICATED_AUTH_TOKEN"
   276→          remoteRef = {
   277→            key      = "crewai-replicated-credentials-${var.environment}"
   278→            property = "auth_token"
   279→          }
   280→        }
   281→      ]
   282→    }
   283→  }
   284→
   285→  depends_on = [
   286→    kubernetes_namespace.crewai_platform,
   287→    module.secret_rails_secrets,
   288→    module.secret_gcs_credentials,
   289→    module.secret_workos_credentials,
   290→    module.secret_github_credentials,
   291→    module.secret_replicated_credentials
   292→  ]
   293→}
   294→

```json
{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/.claude/plans/jolly-percolating-breeze.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Move Hardcoded Secrets to secret.tfvars\n\n## Context\n\nThe dev environment's `secrets.tf` has 4 sets of credentials hardcoded inline (WorkOS, GitHub, Replicated). QA and prod already use the correct pattern: sensitive variables in `variables.tf` with values injected via tfvars at apply time. Dev needs to match that pattern. The `.gitignore` already ignores `secrets.tfvars`.\n\n## Secrets to Extract (dev only)\n\n| Secret | File:Line | Current Value |\n|--------|-----------|--------------|\n| WorkOS client_id | secrets.tf:102 | hardcoded |\n| WorkOS api_key | secrets.tf:103 | hardcoded |\n| WorkOS cookie_password | secrets.tf:104 | hardcoded |\n| WorkOS authkit_domain | secrets.tf:105 | hardcoded |\n| GitHub client_secret | secrets.tf:125 | hardcoded |\n| GitHub app_private_key | secrets.tf:126 | hardcoded |\n| Replicated auth_token | secrets.tf:145 | hardcoded |\n\n## Files to Modify\n\n### 1. `environments/dev/terraform/variables.tf`\nAdd missing sensitive variables (some already exist but are incomplete):\n- `oauth_cookie_password` (new) -- WorkOS cookie password\n- `github_client_secret` (new)\n- `github_app_private_key` (new)\n- `replicated_auth_token` (new)\n\nExisting variables that are already defined but unused in secrets.tf:\n- `oauth_client_id` (line 80, already exists)\n- `oauth_api_key` (line 87, already exists)\n- `oauth_domain` (line 94, already exists)\n\n### 2. `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tf`\nReplace hardcoded values with variable references:\n- Line 102: `\"client_01KCK...\"` → `var.oauth_client_id`\n- Line 103: `\"sk_test_...\"` → `var.oauth_api_key`\n- Line 104: `\"n1roTsn...\"` → `var.oauth_cookie_password`\n- Line 105: `\"undisputed-...\"` → `var.oauth_domain`\n- Line 125: `\"aaa31c7...\"` → `var.github_client_secret`\n- Line 126: `\"-----BEGIN RSA...\"` → `var.github_app_private_key`\n- Line 145: `\"eyJpIjo...\"` → `var.replicated_auth_token`\n\n### 3. `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tfvars` (new, gitignored)\nCreate with the actual secret values extracted from secrets.tf. This file is already covered by `.gitignore` line 18 (`secrets.tfvars`).\n\n### 4. `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tfvars.example` (new)\nTemplate showing required variables without real values. The `.gitignore` already allows `*.example` files (line 107).\n\n## No changes needed for QA/prod\nQA and prod already use `var.oauth_client_id`, `var.oauth_api_key`, `var.oauth_domain` pattern. No hardcoded secrets found there.\n\n## Verification\n```bash\ncd environments/dev/terraform\nterraform init -backend=false\nterraform validate\n# Confirm secrets.tfvars is gitignored\ngit check-ignore secrets.tfvars\n```\n"
}
```

{
  "file_path": "/home/nathan_booth/.claude/plans/jolly-percolating-breeze.md",
  "content": "# Plan: Move Hardcoded Secrets to GitHub Actions Environment Secrets\n\n## Context\n\nThe dev environment's `secrets.tf` has 4 sets of credentials hardcoded inline (WorkOS, GitHub App, Replicated). QA and prod already use the correct pattern: sensitive variables injected via CI/CD. Dev needs to match that pattern. Secrets will be stored as GitHub Actions environment secrets (`dev` environment) and injected as `TF_VAR_*` during applies.\n\n## Secrets to Extract (dev only)\n\n| Variable Name | GitHub Secret Name | Source (secrets.tf) |\n|--------------|-------------------|-------------------|\n| `oauth_client_id` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` | Line 102 |\n| `oauth_api_key` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_API_KEY` | Line 103 |\n| `oauth_cookie_password` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_COOKIE_PASSWORD` | Line 104 |\n| `oauth_domain` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_DOMAIN` | Line 105 |\n| `github_client_secret` | `TF_VAR_GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` | Line 125 |\n| `github_app_private_key` | `TF_VAR_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` | Line 126 |\n| `replicated_auth_token` | `TF_VAR_REPLICATED_AUTH_TOKEN` | Line 145 |\n\n## Steps\n\n### 1. Push secrets to GitHub Actions (environment: dev)\nUse `gh secret set` with `--env dev` for each secret value.\n\n### 2. Add missing variables to `environments/dev/terraform/variables.tf`\n- `oauth_cookie_password` (new)\n- `github_client_secret` (new)\n- `github_app_private_key` (new)\n- `replicated_auth_token` (new)\n\nAlready exist: `oauth_client_id`, `oauth_api_key`, `oauth_domain`\n\n### 3. Replace hardcoded values in `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tf`\nReplace inline strings with `var.*` references.\n\n### 4. Create `secrets.tfvars.example` template\nFor local development reference.\n\n### 5. Validate and commit\n\n## Verification\n```bash\nterraform init -backend=false && terraform validate\ngh secret list --env dev\n```\n"
}
```

## Approved Plan:
# Plan: Move Hardcoded Secrets to GitHub Actions Environment Secrets

## Context

The dev environment's `secrets.tf` has 4 sets of credentials hardcoded inline (WorkOS, GitHub App, Replicated). QA and prod already use the correct pattern: sensitive variables injected via CI/CD. Dev needs to match that pattern. Secrets will be stored as GitHub Actions environment secrets (`dev` environment) and injected as `TF_VAR_*` during applies.

## Secrets to Extract (dev only)

| Variable Name | GitHub Secret Name | Source (secrets.tf) |
|--------------|-------------------|-------------------|
| `oauth_client_id` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_CLIENT_ID` | Line 102 |
| `oauth_api_key` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_API_KEY` | Line 103 |
| `oauth_cookie_password` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_COOKIE_PASSWORD` | Line 104 |
| `oauth_domain` | `TF_VAR_OAUTH_DOMAIN` | Line 105 |
| `github_client_secret` | `TF_VAR_GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET` | Line 125 |
| `github_app_private_key` | `TF_VAR_GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY` | Line 126 |
| `replicated_auth_token` | `TF_VAR_REPLICATED_AUTH_TOKEN` | Line 145 |

## Steps

### 1. Push secrets to GitHub Actions (environment: dev)
Use `gh secret set` with `--env dev` for each secret value.

### 2. Add missing variables to `environments/dev/terraform/variables.tf`
- `oauth_cookie_password` (new)
- `github_client_secret` (new)
- `github_app_private_key` (new)
- `replicated_auth_token` (new)

Already exist: `oauth_client_id`, `oauth_api_key`, `oauth_domain`

### 3. Replace hardcoded values in `environments/dev/terraform/secrets.tf`
Replace inline strings with `var.*` references.

### 4. Create `secrets.tfvars.example` template
For local development reference.

### 5. Validate and commit

## Verification
```bash
terraform init -backend=false && terraform validate
gh secret list --env dev
```

**Not redacted (assessed as non-sensitive):**
- `GITHUB_CLIENT_ID` and `GITHUB_APP_ID` in dev values.yaml — these are public OAuth identifiers, not secrets. The actual secrets (`GITHUB_CLIENT_SECRET`, `GITHUB_APP_PRIVATE_KEY`) are already in Secret Manager via ESO.
- Service account emails, GCP project IDs, internal hostnames — standard infrastructure identifiers, not credentials.
- Team/role email aliases in runbooks — organizational contacts appropriate for internal docs.

**Important caveat**: The redacted values still exist in git history (prior commits). If these credentials haven't already been rotated, they should be. To fully purge from history, `git filter-repo` or BFG Repo-Cleaner would be needed, but that requires force-pushing which rewrites history for all collaborators.
